# Decisions

Status: active register. Owner: Nate. Started 2026-09-12. Written in ASD-STE100.

This file records each owner decision. Each decision has an id (D-#). The numbers never change. A reversed decision stays in this file with a dated note in the "Effect" column. These decisions are the source of the design doc (`docs/design.md`).

How to read this file:

- The "Decision" column gives the answer.
- The "Effect" column gives what the decision changes, binds, or supersedes.
- A date in the "Effect" column marks a later revision.
- The owner answered D-1 to D-24 in the first interview on 2026-09-12. The interview asked each question with options, pros and cons, and a recommended option.
- Three answers, D-5, D-6, and D-7, came as "recommend what is best for this project", and the session chose. The "Decision" column names that.

## Repository and language

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-1 | 2026-09-12 | Implementation language | Rust, with ratatui and crossterm for the terminal front end. The session recommended Go, and the owner chose Rust. | Every tool in the repository is Rust too. Rust is not installed on the dev machine (OQ-2). Revised in part by D-83 on 2026-09-12: ratatui stays, crossterm goes, SDL2 joins. Superseded by D-99 on 2026-09-12: Godot 4 with C#. |
| D-2 | 2026-09-12 | Platforms and CI | macOS, Linux, and Windows terminals at launch. CI runs on hosted GitHub runners for all three. No self-hosted runner. | Three legs per PR. Windows console color and Unicode set the UI floor. Revised in part by D-474 on 2026-09-14, the legs only: five legs per PR, with arm64 Linux and arm64 Windows. The hosted runners and the three systems stand. Revised in part by D-481 on 2026-09-14, the legs and the Mac hardware: three legs again, Linux and Windows on x86_64 and macOS on Apple silicon. D-481 supersedes D-474. |
| D-3 | 2026-09-12 | Build entry | One Makefile with `verify`, `where`, `hooks`, `test`, `lint`, `ste-check`, and `run`. | PR-1 created it. CLAUDE.md names the raw commands too. By 2026-09-20 the file also holds `build`, `format`, `identity`, `content`, `atlas`, and `smoke`, and the audit of D-696 added `atlas` and `run`. |
| D-4 | 2026-09-12 | Repository visibility | The repository stays public. Branch protection on `main` requires the checks. | No personal information in any file, issue, or PR. The `review-gate` check blocks a merge once it exists (OQ-3). Revised in part by D-456 on 2026-09-14: the repository goes private before the first content of a paid region, in Phase 6. Branch protection and the rule on personal information stand. |
| D-5 | 2026-09-12 | Tenets | The six what-you-carry tenets verbatim, plus T-7, deterministic simulation. Conflict order: T-5, T-2, T-3, T-4, T-7, T-1. T-6 is absolute. The owner asked for the best choice, and the session chose T-7, because a replay of any seed makes every combat bug reproducible. | T-7 sits above T-1, so a float convenience never beats determinism. |
| D-6 | 2026-09-12 | Determinism guardrails | All five: integer math only in `core`, one seeded random stream per subsystem, a run record with replay, no clock or OS random in `core`, and a cross-platform state hash job in CI. The owner asked for the best choice, and the session chose all five, because a turn-based game needs no float and the cost is low. | G-2 to G-5. Percentages use fixed-point integers. PR-4 creates the lint and the `replay-identity` job. Revised in part by D-496 on 2026-09-14, the PR of the lint only: PR-46 creates det-lint. PR-4 still creates the `replay-identity` job, and the five guardrails stand. D-504 sets the compare on 2026-09-14: each CI leg checks its state hashes against a committed identity file. |
| D-7 | 2026-09-12 | Content format and strings | Content files are RON, read by serde with unknown fields refused and an absent field as an error. A test loads every content file. Every player-visible string lives in an id-keyed string table, and a lint refuses an inline player string. The owner asked for the best choice, and the session chose RON, because Rust enums map to it directly and it permits comments for balance notes. | G-6, G-7. A JSON export can come later if an external tool needs one. Revised in part by D-116 on 2026-09-12: JSON in place of RON. The string table and the rule on an absent field stand. D-499 sets the form of the lint on 2026-09-14. |
| D-8 | 2026-09-12 | Git rules | Trunk is `main`. Every change starts on a short branch named `<prefix>/pr-<n>-<slug>`. The owner squash-merges. Commit subjects use `feat`, `fix`, `docs`, `test`, or `chore`. A pre-commit hook refuses a commit on `main` and a document that fails the STE check. | G-8, G-15. PR-1 adds the hook and `make hooks`. On 2026-09-14, the owner turned off rebase merging, and `gh api` showed squash merging as the only merge method. Revised in part by D-930 on 2026-09-23, the merge alone: GitHub squash-merges a PR through the auto-merge that the author session turns on, and the owner can still merge. The rest stands. |

## Language and documents

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-9 | 2026-09-12 | Name | The working title `terminal-rpg` stands until a decision. | The crate, the binary, and the repository use it. A rename is one PR (OQ-7). Revised in part by D-102 on 2026-09-12: the world-building interview triggers the rename. |
| D-10 | 2026-09-12 | Writing standard | ASD-STE100 for every document, skill, and agent file, checked in CI on every PR. Dated records are exempt. The interim checker is the Python script from decktome at `docs/tools/ste-check.py`. PR-2 ports it to Rust and retires the script. | G-12. The one Python exception to D-1, until PR-2. Revised in part by D-101 on 2026-09-12: the port is C#, not Rust. Revised in part by D-277 on 2026-09-13: PR-2 writes a new C# checker and ports nothing. The interim script stays until then. PR-2 retired the script on 2026-09-17, and the `ste-check` command of Tools replaced it (D-605). |
| D-11 | 2026-09-12 | Game text voice | The text the player reads has its own voice. STE does not bind it. A `game-text-style` skill records the voice after the owner sets the tone in the roadmap interview. | The string table (D-7) is the place the voice rules apply. |
| D-12 | 2026-09-12 | Terminology | One term per concept. The glossary lives in the `ste-writing` skill. The roadmap interview fills the first entries. | The Rust checker can flag a refused synonym later. |
| D-13 | 2026-09-12 | Id scheme | D-# decisions, OQ-# open questions, F-# findings, G-# guardrails, L-# lessons, T-# tenets, PR-# code changes, M-# measurements. A number never changes and is never reused. | G-10. A PR-# roadmap id and a GitHub PR number differ. |

## Review and process

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-14 | 2026-09-12 | Automated review pass | gitar comments on every PR after a push, before the Codex review. The author answers every comment. When gitar reports a pause, the comment `Gitar review` runs the pass on demand. | gitar is installed, and it ran on PR #1 (F-12). The trial quota pauses the automatic pass, and the comment `Gitar review` runs it. Revised in part by D-895 on 2026-09-22, for the time of the gitar pause: no PR waits for the pass or answers it. The rule stands again at the end of the pause. D-909 ended the pause on 2026-09-23. Revised in part by D-945 on 2026-09-23, for the time of the Gitar pause: no PR waits for the pass, and the author answers each Gitar thread or finding that comes in, after the owner saw it. Revised in part by D-964 on 2026-09-23: a Gitar comment with no thread, no finding, and no claim needs no answer. The answer of each item stands. D-1073 ended the Gitar pause on 2026-09-24. |
| D-15 | 2026-09-12 | Review gate check | A `review-gate` CI check reads `docs/reviews/pr-<number>.md` and verifies the verdict and the effective head. It is a Rust tool in its own early PR, PR-3, and the workflow runs on `pull_request_target` with the head as data only. | Enforced from the start under D-4, once the check exists and the owner requires it (OQ-3). Revised in part by D-101 on 2026-09-12: the tool is C#. D-500 sets how PR-3 meets G-16 on 2026-09-14: a proof in Tests, and a first live run on the next PR. Revised in part by D-579 on 2026-09-16, the scope only: the command also reads the PR description and the changed paths. The review record rules stand. |
| D-16 | 2026-09-12 | Documentation PR override | A PR that changes only documentation merges without a Codex review when the owner adds the `review-override` label. The eligible set that this session applied is `docs/`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.claude/`, and `.github/pull_request_template.md` (OQ-4 asks the owner to confirm the set). | Narrows T-4 for documentation only. A Makefile or a schema always needs the review. Revised in part by D-71 on 2026-09-12, the eligible set only: workflows joined it. Revised in part by D-239 on 2026-09-12, the eligible set only: `README.md` joined it. Revised in part by D-401 on 2026-09-13: a docs PR that adds or revises a row in `docs/decisions.md` takes the review, label or not. Revised in part by D-700 on 2026-09-20: `.claude/settings.json` takes the review, and the rest of the set stands. Revised in part by D-1086 on 2026-09-25: .claude/settings.local.json takes the review too. The rest of the set stands. |
| D-17 | 2026-09-12 | Review skill and record | The what-you-carry `pr-review` skill, adapted to Rust and this project: the provider gate, one review file per PR with a machine-read head and verdict, a response file, and P0 to P3 severity. | `.claude/skills/pr-review/SKILL.md`. Exempt from D-22. |
| D-18 | 2026-09-12 | Session model | One harness invocation is one code PR plus one handoff entry. A documentation PR can follow the merge of that code PR in the same session, with its own entry. The handoff keeps the 10 newest entries with six parts. Older entries move to `docs/session-handoff-archive.md`. The author field reads `Claude Code` or `Codex`. | Every session ends with a push and a check of the remote. Revised in part by D-576 on 2026-09-16, the session model only: one session binds to one PR, and no docs PR follows the merge in the same session. The 10 entries, the six parts, the archive, and the author field stand. |
| D-19 | 2026-09-12 | Open questions | Strict: every open question stops the session. A session files the question in `docs/questions.md` with options and a recommendation, and it picks no default. The session recommended a split rule, and the owner chose strict. | G-9. Drain the questions file before each session. |
| D-20 | 2026-09-12 | Document layout | `docs/design.md` holds the tenets, the guardrails, the finding register, and the high-level roadmap. Focused roadmaps live in `docs/roadmaps/`. `docs/decisions.md`, `docs/questions.md`, `docs/session-handoff.md` with its archive, `docs/reviews/`, and `docs/runbooks/` complete the set. File names in `docs/` are lowercase. `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` are identical. | The read order in `CLAUDE.md`. Revised in part by D-123 on 2026-09-12, the layout only: `docs/world/` joins it. The rest stands. Revised in part by D-403 on 2026-09-14, the layout only: `docs/samples/` joins it, and sessions skip it during automatic exploration. |
| D-21 | 2026-09-12 | Skills and agents on day one | Skills: `ste-writing`, `design-doc-style`, `pr-review`, and `rust-conventions`. Agents: `design-critic` now, and `playtest-bot` once a headless runner exists. | All under `.claude/`. The `playtest-bot` file states that it waits on the runner PR. Revised in part by D-101 on 2026-09-12, the languages: `csharp-conventions` replaces `rust-conventions`. |
| D-22 | 2026-09-12 | Attribution | No agent, harness, or model is named as the source of work in code, game text, commits, PR descriptions, or GitHub comments. Two exemptions: the author field in the session handoff, and the files in `docs/reviews/`. `.claude/settings.json` sets the commit and PR attribution to empty strings. | Absolute, T-6. The harness reminder asks for a trailer in every session, and this rule wins. |
| D-23 | 2026-09-12 | Write scope | This repository takes writes. `~/Repos/decktome` and `/Volumes/SSD-1TB/what-you-carry` are read-only references. No other source. | Binds every session. |
| D-24 | 2026-09-12 | Question format | Ask questions the moment they arise, in small batches, with `AskUserQuestion`. Each question gives its options, the pros and cons of each, and one recommended option marked as such. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-12. Binds every interview. |
| D-25 | 2026-09-12 | Root commit | The repository had no commit at the start of the session. The owner made the root commit on `main` at 14:40 local time, `6b899dd`, with an empty `CLAUDE.md`. Every later change follows D-8. | No session ever commits on `main`. The session put every file on the branch named docs/foundation. |

## Game design

The roadmap interview started on 2026-09-12. Each answer is one row.

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-26 | 2026-09-12 | Push policy (OQ-5) | The foundation documents and the design wait for the end of the roadmap interview, then one PR. After that first PR, a session pushes its branch and opens its PR. | Resolves OQ-5. The first PR is large by design. |
| D-27 | 2026-09-12 | Setting and tone | Dark fantasy. Grim and lethal. | Game text voice (D-11) follows this. Death and loss are part of the design. |
| D-28 | 2026-09-12 | Game structure | Hub settlements, plural, plus branching hand-authored dungeons and a main story. A hub is not always a town: a castle town, a cave community, a boat, an airship. Imagination is key. | Content ships one region at a time. Each hub has its own shape and services. |
| D-29 | 2026-09-12 | Combat model | Turn-based with a visible turn order. Speed decides the order, and haste, slow, and heavy actions shift it. | The timeline is a tactical decision space and a UI element. Fully deterministic (T-7). D-376 sets the model: action delay. |
| D-30 | 2026-09-12 | Scope and length | 20 to 40 hours for a full play. The game ships in slices, and the first slice is one region. | The roadmap has region-level gates. The first playable proves the loop in one region. |
| D-31 | 2026-09-12 | Party size | Three characters in battle. The session recommended four, and the owner chose three. | Faster turns and a tighter UI. One death hurts more, which fits D-27. |
| D-32 | 2026-09-12 | Job model | FF5 style. A character changes jobs freely at a hub. Job levels earn abilities that the character keeps. A character equips one secondary ability set from another job. | The deepest content surface of the game. Balance across combinations is the hardest tuning problem. Revised in part by D-138, D-151, and D-152 on 2026-09-12, the free change only: a change to a hidden job, and its secondary set, need its source. Free change among licensed jobs, kept abilities, and one secondary set stand. Superseded by D-268 on 2026-09-13: the job system leaves the game, and lessons give abilities (D-272, D-278). |
| D-33 | 2026-09-12 | Party members | A fixed cast of story characters. The session recommended a created leader plus recruits, and the owner chose the fixed cast. | The strongest story and the most text. The cast is fixed content. Jobs, not characters, carry the build decision. Revised in part by D-268 and D-272 on 2026-09-13, the build decision only: no job exists, so the build rests on the characters in the party and the lessons they equip. The fixed cast stands. |
| D-34 | 2026-09-12 | Levels | A character level for stats, from experience. A job level per job for abilities, from ability points. A character keeps every learned ability across job changes. | Two progress bars. A rule for a low-level job on a high-level character is an open question. Revised in part by D-268 on 2026-09-13: the job level and its ability points leave the game. The character level from experience stands. How a lesson grows is open (OQ-39). D-357 sets it. D-537 sets where the stats come from on 2026-09-16: a stat curve for each character in content. |
| D-35 | 2026-09-12 | Sources of challenge | Two: enemies with real tactics (focus fire, buffs, interrupts, formations), and scarce resources across a dungeon (limited healing, no free rest). Not chosen: lethal numbers alone, and puzzle bosses as a requirement. | Enemy AI is a large system with its own tests. The retreat decision is real. |
| D-36 | 2026-09-12 | Death and saves | A fallen character stays down until a hub or a rare item revives them. A party wipe reloads the last save point. | A death costs the rest of the dungeon. Save points are content. A short-handed party needs balance. Revised in part by D-231 on 2026-09-12: a wipe reloads the newer of the slot save and the autosave. The rest stands. D-392 lets poison down a character on the map. |
| D-37 | 2026-09-12 | Encounters | Visible enemies on the map. Some are fixed, some patrol. No random encounters. | The map needs enemy movement and sight. Avoidance and ambush are decisions. |
| D-38 | 2026-09-12 | Map view | A top-down tile map drawn with glyphs. The party moves tile by tile. A side panel shows status. | Every dungeon and hub needs a tile layout. Glyph readability sets the terminal size floor. Revised in part by D-106 on 2026-09-12: a scrolling tile map with a camera, and no side panel. |
| D-39 | 2026-09-12 | Dungeon authorship | Hand-authored layouts in content files, with seeded variation in enemies, loot, and some rooms. | A text format for layouts. Each dungeon is a content PR. Revised in part by D-47 on 2026-09-12: the seeded variation is gone, and the hand-authored layout stands. |
| D-40 | 2026-09-12 | Decisions | All four kinds. Story branches with permanent consequences. Tactical decisions in dungeons. Moral choices with a reputation or faction system. Dialogue choices with character relationships. | Four systems: branch flags, resource tension, a reputation model with hub reactions, and a relationship model per character. The text volume is the largest cost of the game. Revised in part by D-329 on 2026-09-13: the reputation model and the relationship model leave. Story branches, tactical decisions, and choices as story flags stand. |
| D-41 | 2026-09-12 | Dungeon parts | All four kinds. Treasure, locked doors and keys, and save points. Traps and hazards. Environmental puzzles: switches, pushable blocks, light and dark. Optional secrets: hidden rooms, secret bosses, lore. | A hazard system, a puzzle mechanic set, and a secret marker in the layout format. D-386 lets a Theft drill open marked locks and disarm traps. |
| D-42 | 2026-09-12 | Magic cost | MP per character. Recovery at hubs, at save points, and by scarce items. | Every cast is a budget decision (D-35). A caster with empty MP needs a no-MP fallback in the job design. Revised in part by D-257 on 2026-09-13: a save point restores MP once per visit to its dungeon. Revised in part by D-268 on 2026-09-13, the effect only: no job design exists, so the action with no MP is open (OQ-44). D-359 sets it: a basic attack for every character. Revised in part by D-555 on 2026-09-16: the save point restores MP once for the place, not once for each visit. |
| D-43 | 2026-09-12 | Elements and status | Six to eight elements with weakness, resist, and absorb. A curated status list of about ten. | Every enemy and item has an affinity row. Each status has its rule in the simulation. The exact lists are open questions. |
| D-44 | 2026-09-12 | Equipment | Six slots: weapon, shield or off-hand, head, body, and two accessories. Job restrictions apply. | Restriction tables are content. A job change can leave a slot empty, and the UI must show it. Revised in part by D-268 on 2026-09-13: no job restrictions exist. The six slots stand, and what limits gear is open (OQ-43). D-374 sets it: nothing. |
| D-45 | 2026-09-12 | Loot | Fixed hand-authored items with a few rarity tiers. No random affixes. No crafting. Consumables were not chosen. | The loot table is finite. The economy is tunable by hand. D-382 limits the pack to small stacks, and D-383 gives each enemy a steal list. Revised in part by D-384 on 2026-09-13: the line on consumables leaves, and a small, hand-placed set of used-up items exists. The rest stands. |
| D-46 | 2026-09-12 | Replayability | Replayability is not a concern for any part of the game. | Owner statement with D-45. Conflicts with the seeded variation of D-39, see OQ-8. Determinism (T-7) stays, because it serves bug reproduction, not replay. |
| D-47 | 2026-09-12 | Dungeon variation (OQ-8) | No seeded variation. Every enemy, chest, and room is placed by hand. The seed drives combat rolls alone. | Resolves the conflict between D-39 and D-46. Revises D-39 in part. The playtest bot varies its inputs, not the layout. |
| D-48 | 2026-09-12 | Terminal size | 120 columns by 40 rows is the floor. Below it the game shows a clear message and no broken screen. The session recommended 80 by 24, and the owner chose 120 by 40. | Every screen designs to 120 by 40. A default macOS Terminal window shows the message on first launch, and the runbook tells the player to resize. Superseded by D-80 on 2026-09-12. |
| D-49 | 2026-09-12 | Color and glyphs | A 256-color palette with a 16-color fallback. Unicode box drawing and a few glyphs with an ASCII fallback. | Two glyph sets and two palettes in content. A test per screen for each. Superseded by D-89 on 2026-09-12: one authored palette, with no terminal fallback. |
| D-50 | 2026-09-12 | Input | Arrow keys and vi keys both. Enter and Escape for menus. Letter hotkeys in battle. No mouse. | The input record holds key presses alone (T-7). Diagonal moves use vi keys. Superseded by D-84 on 2026-09-12: gamepad and keyboard as equals. |
| D-51 | 2026-09-12 | First playable | One hub and one dungeon, with job change and a shop. The session recommended a battle arena alone, and the owner chose the full small loop. | Phase 2 holds the map, the tile format, patrols, battle, the hub screens, and the job screen. It is the largest phase before content. Revised in part by D-268 on 2026-09-13: no job change and no job screen. What the first playable holds in their place is open (OQ-46). D-362 sets it: Marrek, Bergit, and Dagvar. |
| D-52 | 2026-09-12 | Phase gates | A written exit test per phase, and the owner plays the build and signs off on feel. | Each phase gate has a sign-off note in the roadmap with the date. |
| D-53 | 2026-09-12 | Distribution | GitHub Releases with a binary per platform, built by CI on a tag. | A release workflow. The runbook explains the macOS Gatekeeper warning on an unsigned binary. Revised in part by D-85 on 2026-09-12: a Steam release is the goal, and GitHub Releases are the interim. Revised in part by D-455 on 2026-09-14: from PR-40 on, CI signs and notarizes the macOS build. The GitHub Releases on a tag, and the runbook for the unsigned builds before PR-40, stand. D-457 and D-470 set what goes on GitHub Releases. |
| D-54 | 2026-09-12 | License | Public repository, all rights reserved. A `LICENSE` file grants nothing. The session offered a private repository on GitHub Pro, and the owner chose public with no grant. | D-4 stands. Anyone can read the code, and a copy is a copyright violation. Revised in part by D-456 on 2026-09-14: the repository goes private in Phase 6. All rights reserved stood until 2026-09-16. Superseded by D-695 on 2026-09-20: the repository carries the GNU General Public License version 3. |
| D-55 | 2026-09-12 | Jobs at launch | Four jobs at the first playable, eight at the first release. The session proposes the names, and the owner approves. The session recommended six and twelve, and the owner chose four and eight. | Each job is a content PR with abilities, gear rules, and text. Revised in part by D-256 on 2026-09-13: two jobs at the first playable. Superseded by D-268 on 2026-09-13: no job system. |
| D-56 | 2026-09-12 | Region one | Two hubs, four dungeons, one story arc, six to eight hours of play. | About eight content PRs after the systems exist. Region one is the first release (D-30). Revised in part by D-575 on 2026-09-16, the count: five dungeons, with the sealed gallery. The two hubs, the one arc, and the six to eight hours stand. |
| D-57 | 2026-09-12 | Story text | Sessions draft the dialogue, the lore, and the item text under the `game-text-style` skill. The owner approves each batch. | The skill needs the voice rules (D-11, D-27). A wrong tone costs a rewrite PR. |
| D-58 | 2026-09-12 | Cast size | Five characters by the end of region one. Three fight, and two wait in reserve. The party swaps at hubs and at save points. | A downed character (D-36) has a replacement. The reserve needs an experience rule (OQ-9). Revised in part by D-270 on 2026-09-13: more than five characters join over the whole game. D-280 keeps five in region one, and three fight, the reserve, and the swap stand. Revised in part by D-396 on 2026-09-13: the reserve also steps in on the map when poison downs every fighter. The swap at hubs and save points stands. D-397 withdraws that revision on 2026-09-13: the reserve swaps in only at hubs and save points. |
| D-59 | 2026-09-12 | Hub services | All four: rest to full, save, change jobs, and swap the party. A shop that buys and sells. Story scenes, dialogue, and decisions. Side quests and a rumor board. | A quest state system joins the branch flags of D-40. Every hub has a shape of its own (D-28), so not every hub offers every service. Revised in part by D-268 on 2026-09-13: no job change service. Rest, save, the swap, the shop, scenes, and quests stand. |
| D-60 | 2026-09-12 | Economy | Gold from enemies and treasure both. Gold buys gear, items, and rest. The session recommended treasure alone, and the owner chose both. | Every fight pays. The retreat decision (D-35) trades gold for safety. |
| D-61 | 2026-09-12 | Difficulty | One difficulty, tuned by play. No options and no assists. | One tuning target. The playtest sign-off (D-52) is the dial. |
| D-62 | 2026-09-12 | Save files | One slot and one autosave, in the platform config directory. A save holds the run record, so it replays (T-7). The session recommended three slots, and the owner chose one. | The record grows with play, so the format needs a compaction rule. Revised in part by D-258 on 2026-09-13: a one-use resume file joins the two saves. Revised in part by D-259 on 2026-09-13: a load reads the snapshot of the save, not the replay. D-465 names the folder on 2026-09-14: the Godot custom user folder `the-thing-below`. |
| D-63 | 2026-09-12 | Prose voice | Terse and concrete. Short sentences, physical detail, no purple prose, dry understatement. | The `game-text-style` skill records it. OQ-15 asks the owner to approve that skill. |
| D-64 | 2026-09-12 | Bot runner | The headless runner lands right after the battle system, with a random policy and a greedy policy. A few hundred runs per PR, and ten thousand each night. | A nightly workflow and a night gate, as in what-you-carry. The `playtest-bot` agent reads the runner. D-496 puts the night job and the night gate in PR-49 on 2026-09-14, right after the runner of PR-15. Revised in part by D-507 on 2026-09-14, the night count only: ten thousand runs on Linux and two thousand each on Windows and macOS. The runner, the two policies, and the runs per PR stand. D-505 plays the runs on each PR on all three CI legs, and D-509, D-510, and D-513 set the night record and the reach of the gate. |
| D-65 | 2026-09-12 | Enemy AI | Three layers. A tactical evaluator in `core` scores every legal action by its simulated outcome: damage, kills, threat, healing, and timeline shift. ## World-building interview

The world-building interview (OQ-18) started on 2026-09-12, after the merge of PR #1. Each answer is one row.

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-123 | 2026-09-12 | World document home | The world-building output lives in the folder `docs/world/`, one STE file per topic, for example setting, factions, cast, and arc. ## Full plan: technical area

The technical questions of the full plan (D-142, D-146) started on 2026-09-12, after the setting block. Each answer is one row.

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-160 | 2026-09-12 | Renderer (OQ-33) | A Deck test decides. Before PR-1, a throwaway test scene with particles, 2D lights, and a CRT pass runs on the owner's Deck under Forward+ and under Mobile. The owner reads the frame time, and the renderer that holds the target with more room wins. The session recommended this. | Resolves OQ-33 in process. Applies D-92 and G-14. The target is D-161. The pick becomes a decision before PR-1. Where the throwaway scene lives, and how the owner reads the frame time on the Deck, come with the test. Revised in part by D-228 on 2026-09-12: the scene runs at 1280 by 800 and holds the normal maps, shadows, glow, ambient effects, a transition, a backdrop, and the scanlines of D-183 to D-240. D-458 sets the build on 2026-09-14: the test measures the native Linux export. D-523 adds the effect budget to the test on 2026-09-15: the load that still holds 60 frames per second. Revised in part by D-568 on 2026-09-16: the test scene runs at the frame of 1280 by 720. Revised in part by D-599 on 2026-09-16: the pick comes after PR-1, and PR-1 sets Forward+ as a provisional renderer. D-616 on 2026-09-17 gives the answer of the test: the Mobile renderer. D-617 gives the first effect budget. |
| D-161 | 2026-09-12 | Frame rate on the Deck | 60 frames per second, locked, on the Steam Deck with every effect and the CRT on. The session recommended this. | Applies D-92 and D-139. Sets the target of the Deck test (D-160) and of M-6. what-you-carry chose 90 with 60 as a fallback, and this game does not. D-523 holds content inside the target with an effect budget from the Deck test on 2026-09-15. The Deck test of 2026-09-17 held the target in each of its 20 stages under both renderers (D-616). D-618 on 2026-09-17 removed the CRT, so the target reads every effect that the plan holds. |
| D-162 | 2026-09-12 | Map time | The map runs in real time. Patrols walk on a fixed tick whether or not the player moves, and menus pause the world. The session recommended this. | Applies D-37 and the fixed-rate loop of PR-6. The record holds input on every tick (G-5). The tick rate is the next question. D-493 sets what the record holds on 2026-09-14: the intents of each tick. D-522 adds wait intents on 2026-09-15: where the world waits for an effect, Game sends an intent when the effect ends. |
| D-163 | 2026-09-12 | Prologue save in the full game (OQ-28) | The save carries over. A prologue player keeps the party and every choice. The full game imports the last snapshot of the prologue, with its own tests, built on the snapshot of F-10. The session recommended this. | Resolves OQ-28. Applies D-62, D-133, and D-143: Steam Cloud shares the save through the shared cloud app ID. Binds PR-6: the snapshot format survives content changes from the start. The run record of the full game starts at the imported snapshot. D-491 moves the save files, the snapshot versions, and the migrations to PR-43 on 2026-09-14, and PR-6 still makes the snapshot. |
| D-164 | 2026-09-12 | Tick rate | 60 simulation ticks per second on a fixed step. A fixed-step clock in Game calls Core apart from the frame, and a tile step takes a fixed count of ticks. The session recommended this. | Applies D-161, D-162, and G-3: Core has no clock, and the tick is its time. The record holds 60 input samples a second, so the compaction of F-10 matters more. Revised in part by D-493 on 2026-09-14, the effect only: the record holds the intents of each tick, not input samples. The tick rate stands. |
| D-165 | 2026-09-12 | Map authoring | A map is a JSON content file with tile rows as characters, like the sprite grids, plus lists of doors, chests, spawns, and markers. Sessions write the maps, and a tool renders a preview PNG for the owner's approval. The session recommended this. | Applies D-39, D-107, D-116, and G-6. Binds PR-7: the layout format is a grid format. The preview tool joins Tools. D-497 gives the preview tool its own PR, PR-52, on 2026-09-14. D-514 attaches each preview to the PR description on 2026-09-14. |
| D-166 | 2026-09-12 | Save survival | Every content entry has a permanent id that is never reused. A snapshot stores ids and state, never copies of content. Each snapshot format has a version and a migration step, and a test runs each migration on a stored prologue save. The session recommended this. | Applies D-163, and binds PR-5 and PR-6. A content id follows the rule of D-13 for document ids. Every change to the snapshot format adds a migration and a fixture. Revised in part by D-491 on 2026-09-14, the PR only: PR-43 holds the snapshot versions, the migrations, and their test. PR-5 and the content ids stand. |
| D-167 | 2026-09-12 | Languages | English only for the prologue and the launch. The string table, the UI layout, and the font leave room for other languages later. The session recommended this. | Applies G-7 and D-57. Binds the font pick (D-122) and the UI roadmap: a layout leaves room for longer text. |
| D-168 | 2026-09-12 | Shape of Core code | Plain state and systems. State is plain C# records and classes with no engine types. Each system is a static class that takes the state and the intents of one tick, changes the state in a fixed order, and emits events for Game. No framework. The session recommended this. | Applies T-1, G-1, G-4, and G-5. Binds the `csharp-conventions` skill and every Core PR. No entity component system library enters Core. |
| D-169 | 2026-09-12 | Fractions in content | Basis points: an integer where 10000 means 100%. A 2.5% rate is 250, and a 1.5x multiplier is 15000. The session recommended this. | Applies G-2 and D-116. Binds PR-4 and PR-5: the fixed-point type reads basis points from content. |

It shows a short message with the folder path, and the player sends the file by hand. The session recommended this. | Applies T-2, T-7, and D-4: no network and no personal data. Binds PR-6 and the Game layer. A path in the file names no user folder. D-473 names where the file goes on 2026-09-14: a studio email address. Revised in part by D-491 on 2026-09-14, the PR only: PR-44 writes the crash file, through the storage project of D-494. The rest stands. Revised in part by D-559 on 2026-09-16, the PR that shows the message: PR-44 writes the file, and PR-61 shows the message. The file, its content, and the folder path stand. Revised in part by D-1102 on 2026-09-25: the message names the folder in the short form of its system, with no account name. The crash file, its content, and the rest stand. |
| D-171 | 2026-09-12 | Debug tools | Development builds carry a debug console. Each command is an intent in the run record, so a run with a cheat still replays, and the record marks it. A release build holds no debug code, and a test proves it. The session recommended this. | Applies T-7 and D-64: the bots can use the commands. Every command needs an intent type and a test. |

The two baselines, the pinned Mesa, and the contact sheet stand. Revised in part by D-1080 on 2026-09-25: the compare by pixel allows one level on each channel. The pinned Mesa and the baseline of CI stand. |

D-517 puts the record of every content file in Core on 2026-09-14, a file that no rule reads included. |
| D-178 | 2026-09-12 | Save writes | A save writes to a temporary file, checks it, and replaces the old save with a rename in one step. The file carries a checksum, and a load that fails the checksum reports with context. The session recommended this. | Applies D-62 and T-2. Binds PR-6: a test cuts a write in half and proves that the old save survives. Revised in part by D-491 on 2026-09-14, the PR only: PR-43 holds the write and the test, in the storage project of D-494. The rule stands. |
| D-179 | 2026-09-12 | Log format | One JSON object per line: a level, a message, the tick, the subsystem, and its context fields. Core writes no wall-clock time, and Game may add one. The session recommended this. | Applies T-2 and T-7. Binds PR-6 and every later PR that logs. Tools and bots read the logs. what-you-carry made a close choice (its D-212). Revised in part by D-491 on 2026-09-14, the PR only: PR-44 writes the first log file, through the storage project of D-494. Every later PR that logs stays bound. |

## Full plan: graphics area

The graphics questions of the full plan (D-142, D-146) started on 2026-09-12, after the technical area. Each answer is one row.

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |

| D-180 | 2026-09-12 | Effect motion (OQ-30) | The rule "No sub-cell movement" of D-96 binds map sprites alone. Sprites keep tile-locked movement on the map. Effects, and short battle motions such as a lunge or a knockback, move by the pixel inside the 640 by 360 frame. The session recommended this. | Resolves OQ-30. Revises D-96 in part: the rule binds map sprites alone. D-106 stands. The graphics roadmap draws the line between a sprite move and an effect. Revised in part by D-203 on 2026-09-12: map sprites glide between tiles on screen, and Core stays tile-locked. The frame is 1280 by 800 since D-228. Revised in part by D-568 on 2026-09-16: the frame is 1280 by 720. |

The session recommended palette-locked light, and the owner chose a larger palette with free light. | Resolves OQ-31. Revises D-89 in part: the grids pick from the palette, and a lit pixel on screen can hold any color. Revises D-121 in part: 64 in place of 48. Binds PR-34. |

Revised in part by D-266 on 2026-09-13: timings count ticks, not frames. |

Normal maps come from grids (D-184). Whether light touches the UI and the portraits is a question for the UI roadmap. |

The session recommended this. | Applies D-107, D-183, and G-24: the color grid stays the source. The generator and the override format join the atlas tool. D-838, D-839, and D-840 set the height of each color, the override grid, and the rim on 2026-09-21. |

| D-187 | 2026-09-12 | Ambient effects | Region one plans four kinds: snow and wind; fog and mist; fire, embers, and smoke; dust, drips, and motes. Each light source counts against the Deck frame budget (D-161), and the Deck test of D-160 includes the four kinds. |
| D-188 | 2026-09-12 | Glow | A soft glow on light alone: fire, spells, waystones, and the thing below glow a little. Sprites and tiles never glow. With HDR 2D, light brighter than white can glow while sprites and tiles stay clean. Compatibility uses a different glow, so the CI images of D-172 can show a glow unlike the Deck. D-910 on 2026-09-23 picks HDR 2D with a threshold for the first try, and D-912 gives the glow to fire alone in PR-59. |

Superseded by D-442 on 2026-09-14: no day clock runs, and the story sets the time of each map. |
| D-191 | 2026-09-12 | Transitions | A variety of transition effects: shatter, swirl, and at least five or six more. | D-192 | 2026-09-12 | Length of a day | One day lasts 48 minutes of play, in four phases of 12 minutes: dawn, day, dusk, and night, with light blended between them. Every encounter carries a schedule by phase, and the bots test each phase (D-64). Revised in part by D-202 on 2026-09-12: weather stays per map, and the phase changes its light alone. Revised in part by D-257 on 2026-09-13: a phase change does not bring back a killed enemy. Revised in part by D-414 on 2026-09-14: the phase also picks the day or night version of the music. The rest stands. Revised in part by D-436 on 2026-09-14: inside a dungeon, the phase of entry or of the last wait sets routes, sight, enemies, and the wrong things. Revised in part by D-442 on 2026-09-14: the set time of a map, not a phase of a clock, sets its light, routes, sight, and enemies, and the wrong things walk only on a map set to dusk or night. Shops, services, NPCs, and scenes keep no schedule. Revised in part by D-446 on 2026-09-14: the wrong things keep no time rule, and the story places each one. |

Superseded by D-442 on 2026-09-14: no clock runs, and the story sets the time of each map. |

Revised in part by D-268 on 2026-09-13: the jobs window leaves the list. The rest stands. |

Revised in part by D-445 on 2026-09-14: no sun or moon mark shows the time of day, and D-442 supersedes D-192. The health mark stands. |

Revised in part by D-633 on 2026-09-18, the tile count of the frame: the world draws at 2x, so the frame holds 20 by 11.25 tiles. The 32-pixel tile and the 16-pixel font stand. Revised in part by D-707 on 2026-09-19, the font size alone: the body draws at 24 or 32 frame pixels. The 32-pixel tile stands. |
| D-229 | 2026-09-12 | Fit on other screens | Every screen shows exactly the view of the Deck, scaled by the largest whole number that fits, with bars around it. The session recommended this. | Applies D-37 and D-227: nobody sees more of the map than on the Deck. The answer came with a preview of a 640 by 400 frame, and D-228 doubles the frame, so the question comes back under L-6. Revised in part by D-232 on 2026-09-12: the default fits the height, and whole-number scale becomes a setting. Superseded by D-480 on 2026-09-14: the game supports a 16:10 view and a 16:9 view, and every other shape shows black bars. D-568 supersedes D-480 on 2026-09-16: the game draws one 16:9 frame of 1280 by 720. |
| D-230 | 2026-09-12 | Pixel size of the UI | Menus and text draw on the game frame and scale with it, so a text pixel matches an art pixel. The session recommended this. | Settles two texts: D-36 said the last save point, and the plan of PR-16 said the autosave. Revises D-36 in part: the reload target. PR-16 changes with it. Revised in part by D-1114 on 2026-09-25: the pick drops a save of another seed. The newer save of the run still wins. |
| D-232 | 2026-09-12 | Fit on other screens, again | By default, the view of the Deck scales to the full height of the screen: a whole-number upscale, then a smooth fit. Asked again under L-6, because D-228 doubled the frame after D-229. Revised in part by D-480 on 2026-09-14: a 16:9 screen gets the 16:9 view, and every other shape shows black bars around the widest view that fits. The fit to the height and the whole-number scale setting stand. Revised in part by D-568 on 2026-09-16: the fit serves the one 16:9 frame, a desktop at 1920 by 1080 must look good, and OQ-105 holds the method. The two modes stand. Revised in part by D-264 on 2026-09-13: the title font is the bold weight of the body family, not a separate family. |
| D-236 | 2026-09-12 | Enemy sizes at 32-pixel tiles | Common enemies are 32 by 32, elites 64 by 64, and bosses 96 by 96 or larger, which keeps one, two, and three tiles. ## World-building interview: places

The places block of the world-building interview (D-146) started on 2026-09-12, after the UI area. Each answer is one row.

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-242 | 2026-09-12 | Factions with reputation | Four factions track reputation in region one: the officers of the enemy crown, the foreign church, the old faith, and the miners' guild. The session recommended all four. | Applies D-40, D-127, D-137, D-138, and D-153, and binds PR-19. Guards need rules for standing, the old faith needs a way to be found, and a low church standing can bear on papers (OQ-34). Revised in part by D-268 on 2026-09-13, the effect only: OQ-34 closes, and the question of papers moves to stamped rites (OQ-42). Revised in part by D-329 on 2026-09-13: no faction tracks reputation. The four factions stand as the powers of region one. |
| D-243 | 2026-09-12 | Hub shapes of region one | A walled mining town in a pass, held by the enemy crown and the foreign church, and a cave community where the old faith hides. The session recommended this. | Applies D-28, D-56, D-137, and D-153. The two hubs show the ban: the law above, the faith below. The airship waits for a later region (D-129). The two hubs need distinct looks, because both are stone and dark. |
| D-244 | 2026-09-12 | The four dungeons of region one | The deep mine, the border fort, the hanging cells, and the ice crossing. The session recommended the deep mine, the border fort, the barrow, and the ice crossing, and the owner chose the hanging cells in place of the barrow. | Applies D-56, D-127, D-149, D-153, and D-155. The hanging cells sit under the chapel of the mining town, a dungeon inside a hub, which the single map code path of D-112 must allow. The border fort stays clear of the banned rebel brigade (D-140). Revised in part by D-575 on 2026-09-16: the sealed gallery is the fifth dungeon. |
| D-245 | 2026-09-12 | Region map shape | Two valleys joined by one pass: a hub and two dungeons in each valley, and one pass route that the story opens. The session recommended this. | Applies D-113. The owner then refused both placements of hubs and dungeons in the valleys, because a mine, a mining town, and caves do not belong on a lower valley floor. The layout comes back as a question. Superseded by D-250 on 2026-09-13: the layout follows the mountain, with the caves across a gorge from the town. |
| D-246 | 2026-09-12 | Hidden sources of region one | Both hidden sources sit in the cave community: an old-faith hexer and a cutpurse's fence. The session recommended this. | Applies D-150 and D-151. One trip finds both, and the hub of the banned faith is the place outside the law. The cave community becomes the hub for changes to hidden jobs, which softens the rarity of D-138. Revised in part by D-268 on 2026-09-13: the two teach no job. The old-faith hexer and the fence still live in the cave community, and what they offer is open (OQ-47). D-365 sets it: people in the story teach or give lessons. |
| D-247 | 2026-09-12 | Power in the mining town | The miners' guild runs the mines and the streets, a crown governor takes the tax, and the foreign church holds the chapel and the license office. The session recommended this. | Applies D-242 and D-243: three factions in one hub, each with a hand on the party. |
| D-248 | 2026-09-12 | People of the cave community | Believers of the old faith lead the cave community, and hexers, deserters, and debtors shelter there on its terms. The session recommended this. | Applies D-137, D-149, D-243, and D-246. The refuge is not purely devout, which colors the doubt of D-137. |
| D-249 | 2026-09-12 | Why the bandits roam (OQ-36) | The passes pay: tolls, ore trains, and travelers make banditry a trade. The mining town sits in a high valley beside its deep mine, with the hanging cells under its chapel. The cave community lives in the cliff across a gorge from the town, reached by a crossing the law cannot hold. The ice crossing leads over the high pass and out of the region. The session recommended this. | Supersedes D-245: no two valleys. Applies D-150, D-153, D-243, D-244, and D-246: the hidden sources lie near enough to find early. The crossing is a route that the story controls. Revised in part by D-255 on 2026-09-13: the border fort stands at the high pass. Revised in part by D-268 on 2026-09-13, the effect only: the two people of D-246 teach no job. Revised in part by D-371 on 2026-09-13: a small village sits on the road in, below the high valley. |
| D-251 | 2026-09-13 | The gorge crossing | A mine gallery from before the war runs under the gorge. The guild sealed it, and the old faith keeps the far end. The session recommended this. | Applies D-113, D-242, D-248, and D-250. The sealed door is a story gate on the region map. |
| D-252 | 2026-09-13 | Bandits in the border fort | The treaty bars soldiers from a strip along the new border, so neither crown may garrison the border fort, and bandits took it to toll the road. The session recommended this. | Applies D-127, D-244, and D-249. The crown needs a reason not to break its own treaty. D-255 puts the fort at the high pass, where the new border runs. |

D-442 supersedes D-192 on 2026-09-14, and the season stands. |

The session recommended the enemy crown beyond the pass, and the owner chose the old crown. | Applies D-127 and D-250. The ice crossing leads toward the crown that signed the region away, and region two meets it. The supply road of the occupier runs up from the foothills. |

The fort and the ice crossing both sit at the top, so the first dungeon of the region is another place. D-252 stands on the new border. D-313 sets the first dungeon: the hanging cells. |
| D-256 | 2026-09-13 | Jobs at the first playable | The first playable holds two jobs: the Warden and the Mender. The Hexer and the Cutpurse wait for the cave community. Answers C-1 of the design critic (F-25). PR-12 and PR-17 change with it. Superseded by D-268 on 2026-09-13: no job system. What the first playable holds is open (OQ-46). |
| D-257 | 2026-09-13 | Rest at a save point | A save point restores MP once per visit to its dungeon, and a killed enemy stays dead until the party leaves the dungeon. The session recommended this. | Revises D-42 in part: recovery at a save point is once per visit. Revises D-193 in part: a phase change does not bring back a killed enemy. Applies D-35 and D-198. Both rules need tests. Answers C-2 (F-25). D-389 keeps health out of the restore. Revised in part by D-442 on 2026-09-14: no phase changes happen, and a change of the set time by the story does not bring back a killed enemy. D-442 supersedes D-198. Revised in part by D-555 on 2026-09-16: a killed enemy stays dead past the exit, and the MP restore runs once for the place, until a story event reopens it. |
| D-258 | 2026-09-13 | The quit save | Quitting writes a resume file, not the autosave. A load of the resume file deletes it, so a wipe never reloads it. The session recommended this. | Revises D-224 in part: the autosave no longer writes on quit. Revises D-62 in part: a resume file joins the slot save and the autosave. D-231 stands. Answers C-3 (F-25). |
| D-259 | 2026-09-13 | Loading a save after a patch | Every save writes a full snapshot at its tick, and a load reads that snapshot alone. The run record keeps the inputs for a bug replay on the version that made them. The session recommended this. | Answers C-4 of the design critic (F-25). Revises D-62 in part: a load reads the snapshot, not the replay. Applies D-163, D-166, and G-17. A crash file names its build version (D-170), because a replay of an old record needs the old build. PR-6 changes with it. D-448 sets the format of the version on 2026-09-14. Revised in part by D-491 on 2026-09-14, the PR only: PR-43 holds the load of the snapshot. The rule stands. D-1110 to D-1112 set on 2026-09-25 what a load does after a patch. The rule stands. |

Revises D-118 in part: a fifth project, the debug assembly, joins the solution. Core gains an explicit seam for extra intent handlers. The technical roadmap names the PR that creates the assembly. D-492 names the PRs on 2026-09-14: PR-6 adds the seam, and PR-45 creates the assembly right after PR-7. |
| D-261 | 2026-09-13 | A missed Deck target | No fallback is set in advance. If the Deck test of D-160 cannot hold 60 frames per second, the owner decides then. | Owner answer, 2026-09-13. Applies D-160 and D-161, and leaves the fallback of F-26 open by choice. |
| D-262 | 2026-09-13 | The place of systems, audio, and release | They come after the arc: places, then the cast, then the arc, then systems, audio, and release. The session recommended this. | Revises D-146 in part: the order gains its last three areas. Answers C-11 of the design critic (F-27). |

The family, the license, and the hard pixel edge stand. |

## World-building interview: the cast

The cast block of the world-building interview (D-146, D-262) started on 2026-09-13, after the places block. The owner replaced the job system during it (D-268, D-272). Each answer is one row.

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-267 | 2026-09-13 | The lead | One character of the cast is the lead. Scenes center on the lead, and the dialogue choices of the player are the lines of the lead. The session recommended this. | Applies D-33, D-40, and D-114. The cast block asks next who the lead is. |
| D-268 | 2026-09-13 | No job system | The job system leaves the game. Each character has a name and a fixed role from the start, and a side role that a story event unlocks in play. The party grows as the lead meets these characters through the first part of the game. One character at most holds each kind of role: one main healer and one side healer. The owner gave this in place of an answer on why the party travels together. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-13: "This is a significant reframing of our game. Think about it carefully." Supersedes D-32, D-55, D-77, D-150, D-151, D-152, and D-256. Revises D-33, D-34, D-44, D-59, D-138, D-211, and D-246 in part. Revised in part by D-272 and D-274 on 2026-09-13: aptitudes and ability items take the place of roles. The parts that stand: no job change, a party that grows by meetings, a second trait that a story event unlocks, and one character per kind. |
| D-269 | 2026-09-13 | Origins of the cast | Four characters come from region one, and one comes from the land of the enemy crown. The session recommended three and two. | Applies D-127, D-137, and D-159. The answer counted a cast of five. Revised in part by D-273 on 2026-09-13: the mix binds region one alone. |
| D-270 | 2026-09-13 | A loss in the cast | The story kills or removes a character at a set point, and the player has no choice in it. Another story character must be able to replace what the lost character did. More than five characters can join the party over the game. The session recommended that all five survive region one, and the owner chose a set loss with these two conditions. | Revises D-58 in part: more than five characters, and three still fight (D-31). Applies D-27 and D-135. D-271 and D-274 set what a replacement shares. Where the loss falls belongs to the arc block. Revised in part by D-279 on 2026-09-13: the loss is one death. |
| D-271 | 2026-09-13 | What a replacement holds | The rule of one character per kind counts every character in the whole game, so no two characters ever share a role. A replacement brings other roles that cover the same need. The session recommended that a replacement join after the loss with the same pair of roles, and the owner chose a close match that is never the same. | Applies D-268 and D-270. Revised in part by D-274 on 2026-09-13: a replacement can share the main aptitude, and its side aptitude differs. The part that stands: a replacement is never the same as the character it replaces. |
| D-272 | 2026-09-13 | Ability items in place of classes | No classes. Abilities come from a system in the shape of the materia of Final Fantasy VII, where any character can equip the items that give abilities. Characters differ in what they do best: some heal better, and some fight better in melee. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-13, in place of an answer on the count of roles. Revises D-268 in part: aptitudes and ability items take the place of fixed roles. Applies D-45. The session advised that no item copies itself, and the systems block asks it. D-275 keeps the item clear of the banned stones of D-140. |
| D-273 | 2026-09-13 | Origins past five | In region one, about four local characters join for each character from the enemy crown. Characters who join in later regions can come from anywhere, the old crown included. The session recommended exactly one foreigner in the whole cast. | Revises D-269 in part: the mix binds region one alone. Applies D-254 and D-270. Each later region sets its own mix. |
| D-274 | 2026-09-13 | Aptitudes | Any character can equip any ability item. Each character has a main aptitude, the kind of ability that character does best, and a story event unlocks a side aptitude. No two characters share a main aptitude, but a replacement for a dead character can share the main aptitude of that character. No two characters share a side aptitude, and the side aptitude of a replacement differs from that of the dead character. The session recommended the rule of one per kind on both aptitudes with no exception. | Revises D-271 in part: a replacement can share the main aptitude. Applies D-268, D-270, and D-272. The session reads "depending on character deaths" as a share through a replacement alone. How an aptitude changes the numbers is a question for the systems block. D-282 sets the story event: a personal task. D-303 confirms the reading. |
| D-275 | 2026-09-13 | Form of an ability item | A spell is a rite, written on a page or scratched on a strip of soft metal. A physical ability is a drill, a written form for a fight or a theft. The item teaches a form and holds no power, because every caster draws on the thing below (D-148). The session recommended this. | Applies D-129, D-130, D-140, and D-148. The license law reaches the rites a character uses: a rite with a church stamp is legal, and a rite with no stamp can hang its user. No stone or orb holds power (D-140). Revised in part by D-302 on 2026-09-13: a legal use needs a license on the caster and a stamp on the rite. |
| D-276 | 2026-09-13 | The four job names | Warden, Hexer, Mender, and Cutpurse name kinds of people in the story and the law. No menu shows them as a class. The session recommended this. | Revises D-76 in part: the four names stay as words of the world, not jobs. Revises D-233 in part: the four test sprites need new identities. The word warden also names the church wardens among the enemies (D-155), so the world files keep its uses apart. |
| D-277 | 2026-09-13 | Other repositories | The other repositories guide the documents alone. No code, tool, or game system comes from another repository. PR-2, PR-3, PR-4, PR-5, PR-15, and PR-38 write their code new in this repository from the rules that the documents state. The interim STE checker stays until PR-2 replaces it with a new tool. The session recommended this. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-13: "I don't want to COPY things from other repos. I meant to use those other repos as guidance for DOCS ONLY." Applies D-23. Revises D-10, D-101, D-115, and D-116 in part: no port. A session reads another repository for its documents and process, never for its code. D-496 moves det-lint to PR-46 and the night gate to PR-49 on 2026-09-14, and each still writes new code. |
| D-278 | 2026-09-13 | Lesson | A lesson is anything a character equips to gain an ability: a rite or a drill. The documents use lesson, main aptitude, and side aptitude as terms. Menus can show other words under the game voice. The session recommended this. | Applies D-12, D-272, D-274, and D-275. The glossary of the `ste-writing` skill gains the three terms, and the job terms leave it. |
| D-279 | 2026-09-13 | Deaths in the cast | The story kills one character in the whole game, and one replacement joins. The session recommended two or three. | Applies D-270 and D-274. The words "depending on character deaths" in D-274 mean this one death. Where it falls belongs to the arc block. D-309 puts it after region one, and D-321 names Elio. |
| D-280 | 2026-09-13 | Characters in region one | Five characters join during region one: four local and one from the enemy crown. The session recommended this. | Applies D-58, D-270, and D-273. D-58 stands for region one, and more characters join in later regions. The replacement joins after region one (D-299), and where the death falls belongs to the arc block (OQ-48). D-309 puts the death after region one. |
| D-281 | 2026-09-13 | Aptitude kinds | Eight kinds, four for rites and four for drills. Rites: mend (healing), harm (damage spells), blight (poison, sleep, and other statuses), and boon (shields and haste). Drills: blade (melee), guard (protect allies), shot (crossbow and bow), and theft (steal, locks, and traps). A side aptitude never matches the main aptitude of the same character. The session recommended this. | Applies D-74, D-75, D-274, and D-278. The whole cast, the replacement included, holds eight characters at most, because no two share a side aptitude. Five join in region one (D-280), so later regions add three at most. If the replacement shares the main aptitude of the dead character, at least one kind is never the main aptitude of any character (D-303). D-394 puts the rites that cure afflictions in Mend. |
| D-282 | 2026-09-13 | What unlocks a side aptitude | A personal task. Each character has a side task in the hubs, and the side aptitude opens when the party finishes it. A player can miss a task and play on without that side aptitude. The session recommended a scene in the main story. | Applies D-59 and D-274. Balance must hold with any side aptitude absent. The quest state of PR-19 carries the tasks. D-352 puts the content of each task in PR-28 and PR-29. D-375 closes a missed task at the end of its region. |
| D-283 | 2026-09-13 | A side aptitude before it unlocks | Hidden. The menu shows an empty mark until the task unlocks the side aptitude. The session recommended this. | Applies D-282. The player cannot plan a party around a side aptitude before its task ends. |
| D-284 | 2026-09-13 | Who the lead is | A young local commoner who grew up in the mining town under the ban. The session recommended this. | Applies D-154, D-243, D-267, and D-280. The lead is one of the four local characters of region one. The class war looks up from below (D-125), and the story brings church and crown politics to the lead. Revised in part by D-368 on 2026-09-13: the lead grew up in a small village near the mining town. The young local commoner under the ban stands. |
| D-285 | 2026-09-13 | The main aptitude of the lead | Blade. The lead fights up close with what a miner carries. The session recommended mend, with the lead as a hexer by law. | Applies D-281 and D-284. No other character of region one has blade as a main aptitude (D-274). The law touches the lead less than it touches a caster (D-130). |
| D-286 | 2026-09-13 | The gender of the lead | Fixed. A later batch sets it with the name of the lead. The session recommended this. | Applies D-33 and D-267. One portrait, one sprite set, and one form of each line that names the lead. |
| D-287 | 2026-09-13 | Why each character stays | Each character has a reason of their own that lies on the road of the lead, such as a debt to pay, a person to find, or a hanging to escape. The four factions stay open to the choices of the player. D-315 adds a reason to follow the party out of region one. |
| D-288 | 2026-09-13 | The foreign character of region one | A clerk of the license office, who stamps rites for the foreign church. The session recommended a deserter, and the owner chose the clerk. | Applies D-130, D-247, D-275, and D-280. The law argues inside the party. The clerk has every reason to report the party, so the reason to stay (D-287) must be strong. |
| D-289 | 2026-09-13 | The other four of region one | Three local characters: a guild warden with the guard aptitude, a hexer with the harm aptitude, and a cutpurse with the theft aptitude. The four test sprites become the warden, the hexer, the clerk, and the cutpurse. A legal healer and an illegal caster share the party. Boon, blight, and shot are the main aptitude of no character in region one, and the one foreigner holds the only main mend. |
| D-290 | 2026-09-13 | Why the clerk stays | The church or the governor sends the clerk to travel with the party and report. The clerk stays as a watcher who can turn. Which power sends the clerk, and when the player learns it, belong to the arc block. D-316 sets the sender on 2026-09-13: the foreign church, after the battle in the cells. The clause on the relationship and the reports conflicts with D-317. Revised in part by D-328 on 2026-09-13, the effect only: no relationship decides what the clerk reports, and no choice near the clerk tests loyalty. The sender of D-316 and the watcher who can turn stand. |
| D-291 | 2026-09-13 | Why the lead leaves | A parent of the lead went down into the deep mine and did not come back. The lead goes down to learn why. What happened to the parent belongs to the arc block. D-311 sets what region one reveals: the sealed crew, without the parent. |
| D-292 | 2026-09-13 | The lead in battle | The lead can wait in reserve like any other character, but the map always follows the lead. The session recommended that the lead always fight. | Owner answer, 2026-09-13. Revises D-106 in part: the camera follows the lead, not the party leader. Applies D-58 and D-73: a lead in reserve earns half experience. The session reads this as the lead walking the map in every case, in the party or in reserve. D-306 confirms the reading on 2026-09-13. |
| D-293 | 2026-09-13 | Side aptitudes of region one | The lead hides guard, the warden shot, the hexer blight, the cutpurse blade, and the clerk boon. The session recommended mend for the hexer and blight for the cutpurse. | Applies D-274, D-281, D-282, D-283, and D-289. All eight kinds reach region one. Every side aptitude of a later character is harm, mend, or theft, because no two characters share a side aptitude. |
| D-294 | 2026-09-13 | Why the warden stays | The guild took the mark of the warden for refusing an order in the mines. The warden knows something the guild hides, and the search of the lead needs it. The session recommended this. | Applies D-247, D-276, D-287, and D-291. The warden has no standing left with the guild, so the guild reaches the party through a grudge. What the order was belongs to the arc block. D-311 sets it: to seal a level with a crew still inside. D-338 sets what she knows: the seal, the lie, and the place, but not what waits below or who was in the crew. |
| D-295 | 2026-09-13 | Why the hexer stays | The hexer keeps the old-faith rites at the waystones that the church breaks, and the road of the lead passes the stones. The session recommended this. | Applies D-134, D-141, D-287, and D-289. The personal task of the hexer can grow from the rites. The mystery of the stones pays off at the end of the game (D-131), so this reason pays off slowly. |
| D-296 | 2026-09-13 | Why the cutpurse stays | The cutpurse smuggles goods through old mine tunnels and knows ways into the deep mine. The lead needs the way, and the cutpurse needs the blade of the lead. The session recommended this. | Applies D-244, D-287, D-289, and D-291. The reason of a guide ends when the lead reaches the bottom, so a second reason must grow. D-349 sets it: loyalty she did not expect. |
| D-297 | 2026-09-13 | Names and genders of region one | The lead is Marrek, a man. The warden is Bergit, a woman. The hexer is Dagvar, a man. The cutpurse is Ottild, a woman. The four local names have two syllables each and sound hard and closed, and the foreign name Elio sounds soft and open. Two-syllable names make battle messages longer (rule 8). D-300 lets Elio take three syllables. |
| D-298 | 2026-09-13 | Ages of region one | Marrek is 19, Ottild 21, Elio 24, Bergit 31, and Dagvar 35. The session recommended 19, 20, 27, 44, and 66. | Applies D-126 and D-154. Every character of the party is an adult, because the session reads D-126 as a limit on wounds in battle too. Bergit and Dagvar were adults when the ban came, so they remember the faith before it. Nobody in the party remembers the time before the war, so the old faith has a thinner voice. D-305 confirms the reading on 2026-09-13, for the whole cast. |
| D-299 | 2026-09-13 | Size of the whole cast | Eight characters in the whole game: the five of region one, and two new characters and the replacement, who join after region one. If the replacement shares the main aptitude of the dead character, one kind is never a main aptitude. The three later side aptitudes are harm, mend, and theft, and the arc sets the death before them, because some pairs leave the replacement no legal side aptitude. At most seven characters live at one time. |
| D-300 | 2026-09-13 | Syllables of a foreign name | Rule 11 of the `game-text-style` skill gains an exception: the name of a foreign person can take three syllables. | D-301 | 2026-09-13 | A lost ally | A choice of the player never removes a cast member. | D-302 | 2026-09-13 | What makes the use of a rite legal | Two things: the caster holds a church license, and the rite carries a church stamp. A use of a rite without either breaks the law. OQ-42 asks how a character gets a license and a stamp. D-366 sets it: the party never gets one. |
| D-303 | 2026-09-13 | Who shares a main aptitude | The replacement alone can share the main aptitude of the dead character. The session recommended this. | Confirms the reading that D-274 records, and answers C-7 of the second critic pass (F-28). Applies D-279 and D-299: the two new characters take two of boon, blight, and shot as main aptitudes. The replacement can also take a main aptitude of its own. |

| D-306 | 2026-09-13 | The map sprite of a lead in reserve (OQ-53) | The lead walks the map in every case, in the party or in reserve. The session recommended this. | Resolves OQ-53, and confirms the reading that D-292 records. Applies D-106 and D-267. One sprite walks the map, and it can belong to a character who does not fight.
