## World-building interview: the arc

The arc block of the world-building interview (D-146, D-262) started on 2026-09-13, after the answers to the second critic pass. Each answer is one row.

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-309 | 2026-09-13 | The death and the lead (OQ-48) | The one death in the cast falls after region one, in a later region. The lead never dies. The session recommended this. | Resolves OQ-48. Applies D-163, D-267, D-279, D-280, and D-299. All five characters of region one live at the end of the prologue, and its save carries all five. D-58, D-267, and D-292 stand. Who dies, and in which region, come in a later question of the arc block. |
| D-310 | 2026-09-13 | The face of the users in region one | Nobody. Region one shows the harm that the users of the thing below leave, and it never shows who they are. The session recommended the master of the miners' guild, and the owner chose no face. | Applies D-128 and D-291: the mystery lasts past the prologue. The bosses of region one are bandits, church wardens, and wrong things (D-155). D-319 adds the bishop of the mining town and his soldiers. Who the users are waits for a later region (D-131). |
| D-311 | 2026-09-13 | What the guild hides | A crew of miners broke into the old ground, and the guild sealed the level with the crew still inside. Bergit refused the order to seal it. In the deep mine, Marrek opens the level and finds the crew, but not his parent. The session recommended this. | Answers the parts of D-291 and D-294 that region one reveals. Applies D-128, D-244, and D-247. One crime ties the lead to the warden, and the guild is guilty from early on. The fate of the parent stays open after region one (D-291). Revised in part by D-324 on 2026-09-13, the effect only: the guild is neither evil nor good. The seal, the refusal, and the finds stand. |
| D-312 | 2026-09-13 | How much of the thing below region one shows | A large wrong thing, born where blood spilled, holds its area at the bottom of the deep mine as a boss. The session recommended one answer of the thing below with no shape, and the owner chose the boss. | Applies D-128, D-155, D-206, D-209, and D-236. A fight carries the reveal, and the bosses of PR-20 get a source. The creature stays clear of a stock demon (D-128). Whose blood made it is the next question. D-323 sets it: blood of the war. |
| D-313 | 2026-09-13 | The order of the dungeons | The party plays the four dungeons of region one in this order: the hanging cells, the deep mine, the border fort, and the ice crossing. The session recommended this. | Applies D-244, D-250, and D-255, and settles the first dungeon that D-255 left open. The first playable builds the mining town and the hanging cells (PR-17), and D-369 adds the village. The sealed crew and its boss land in the middle of the region (D-311, D-312), so the fort and the crossing need weight of their own. Revised in part by D-575 on 2026-09-16: the order becomes the hanging cells, the deep mine, the sealed gallery, the border fort, and the ice crossing. |
| D-314 | 2026-09-13 | Why the party leaves region one | Two reasons together. The party leaves hunted: it knows the crime of the guild, and the guild wants the witnesses silent. The party also follows a trace of Marrek's parent: the sealed level shows that the parent left it alive, and the trail leads over the pass. The session recommended the hunt alone, and the owner joined it to the trace. | Applies D-252, D-253, D-254, D-291, and D-311. Region two opens with the party in flight from region one, and the search of the lead pulls it on. D-319 adds the foreign church to the hunt, and D-324 makes the guild neither evil nor good, so the part of the guild in the hunt is a question. Revised in part by D-332 on 2026-09-13: church wardens hunt the party, and the guild lets the witnesses go. The flight and the trace of the parent stand. D-344 sets the trace. |
| D-315 | 2026-09-13 | A reason to follow over the pass | Each character needs a reason to follow the party out of region one. The reason can be personal, loyalty to the party from shared experience, anger at the enemy crown or the foreign church, or a reason not yet set. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-13, with D-314. Applies D-287 and D-296: the reason to stay on the road of the lead grows into a reason to leave the region. The reason of each character is a question for the arc block. D-347, D-348, and D-349 set the reasons of Bergit, Dagvar, and Ottild. D-320 gives the reason of Elio, and D-344 the trail of Marrek. |
| D-316 | 2026-09-13 | Who sends Elio | The foreign church sends Elio after the battle in the hanging cells. He says that he comes to help the party in the deep mine, and he spies on it. The owner gave this in place of the options. | Settles the sender that D-290 left open: the foreign church, not the governor. Elio joins between the first and the second dungeon (D-313), and the party does not know that he spies. The motive of the church is a question, because the owner asked for one. D-325 sets it: the refuge of the old faith. |
| D-317 | 2026-09-13 | Elio in the deep mine | When Elio sees what lives below, he grows discontent with his task. He does not confess to the party, and he does not send his report to the church office when it is due. The owner gave this. | Applies D-312 and D-316. The clause of D-290 that the relationship decides what Elio reports conflicts with this set turn, and a question asks which holds. D-328 settles it: the relationship side leaves, and the set turn holds. |
| D-318 | 2026-09-13 | The capture | The foreign church confronts the party with armed soldiers and a priest. The priest tells the party that Elio spied on them. The church captures the party and Elio, and it imprisons Elio with them, because he did not report what he found. The owner gave this. | Applies D-316 and D-317. The owner wrote "imprinted", and the session reads "imprisoned" and asks the owner to confirm. Where the capture happens, and whose soldiers these are, are questions. D-327 keeps the reading and sets the place: the hanging cells. D-332 sets the soldiers: church wardens. |
| D-319 | 2026-09-13 | The breakout and the bishop | The party, Elio included, breaks free and fights the local bishop of the foreign church and his soldiers. Elio becomes sure that the church does not keep its stated purpose. The bishop and his soldiers die, and the party flees for the high pass. The owner gave this. | Applies D-131: the bishop dies, and the foreign church stands. Applies D-137: Elio sees what the church does, and no text proves or disproves its god. The bishop is a new figure of the mining town (D-247), and nothing in region one shows him as a user of the thing below (D-310). The party then plays the border fort and the ice crossing (D-313). |
| D-320 | 2026-09-13 | Elio stays | On the road to the pass, Elio has a long and hard talk with the party. He declares his loyalty to the party and to the goal to stop the foreign church. The party trusts him with reluctance and lets him stay. The owner gave this. | Applies D-287, D-288, D-301, and D-315: loyalty and a cause against the church are the reasons Elio stays. The talk stays clear of a climb to power through betrayal (`docs/world/banned-devices.md`). |
| D-321 | 2026-09-13 | Who dies | Elio. After region one, he dies in a fight against the foreign church, and his death proves to the party that he was on their side. The owner gave this. | Applies D-279 and D-309. If the replacement shares Mend, the main aptitude of Elio, its side aptitude is Harm or Theft (D-274, D-299, D-303). What happens to the lessons and the gear of Elio is open (OQ-41). The region of the death belongs to the arc of that region. |
| D-322 | 2026-09-13 | Who Elio is | Elio is an innocent type: lovable, a character the player loves. The owner gave this as a firm rule. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-13: "Elio MUST be an innocent type, lovable, a character the user/player will love." Binds every scene, line, portrait, and sprite of Elio (D-57, G-20). |
| D-323 | 2026-09-13 | The blood that made the boss | The wrong thing at the bottom of the deep mine was there before the crew: blood spilled in the war made it. The session recommended the blood of the breach, and the owner chose the war. | Applies D-128, D-154, and D-312: the war that woke the thing below left the creature in the ground. |
| D-324 | 2026-09-13 | The moral shade of the guild | The miners' guild is morally ambiguous: neither evil nor good. The owner gave this with D-323. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-13. Revises the effect of D-311 in part: the guild is not plainly guilty. The seal of the crew and the refusal of Bergit stand. Why the guild sealed the level is a question. Binds the guild in every region (D-131, D-242). D-326 sets the reason: to keep the wrong thing below, and then a lie. |
| D-325 | 2026-09-13 | The motive of the foreign church | The church believes that the party can lead it to the refuge of the old faith, through the sealed gallery under the gorge, and the deep mine is the way in. The session recommended the mines of the guild, and the owner chose the refuge. | Answers the question of D-316. Applies D-137, D-149, D-248, and D-251. The church hunts the old faith and wants what lies past the mine, so the sealed crew stays the story of the guild. Region one shows no use of the thing below by the church (D-310). |
| D-326 | 2026-09-13 | Why the guild sealed the level | The guild sealed the level to keep the wrong thing below, with the crew still inside. Then it called the loss a collapse, to keep the ore trains and the tax moving. The session recommended the seal to save the town, and the owner chose the seal and the lie. | Answers the question of D-324. Applies D-247, D-249, D-311, and D-323. A hard choice and a cover-up keep the guild neither evil nor good. The collapse story covers what the guild hides (D-294, D-311). |
| D-327 | 2026-09-13 | Where the capture happens | A second visit to the hanging cells. The party wakes in the cells, breaks out through a changed layout, and fights the bishop in the chapel above. The owner kept the reading "imprisoned" of D-318. The session recommended scenes and one set battle, and the owner chose the second visit. | Answers the questions of D-318 and D-319. Applies D-244 and D-313. Region one keeps four dungeons, and the first dungeon returns with a second layout, new enemies, and the bishop as a boss. The roadmaps give the second visit its PR. D-575 on 2026-09-16 makes the count five dungeons, with the sealed gallery. This answer stands. |
| D-328 | 2026-09-13 | Relationships leave | The relationship side of the game leaves as a whole, for more static story decisions. The owner gave this in answer to the conflict of D-290 and D-317. The session recommended a set turn for Elio, with lines that change with the relationship. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-13: "Drop the 'relationships with people and parties' side of things as a whole. More static story decisions." Revises D-290 in part: no relationship decides what Elio reports, and the set turn of D-317 holds. What the words "people and parties" cover in D-40 and D-242 is the next question. D-329 sets it. |
| D-329 | 2026-09-13 | What leaves with the relationships | The relationship value per character and the reputation per faction both leave. A choice stays as a fixed story flag: it sets a flag, and a scene, a route, or a hub reads it. The session recommended this. | Answers the question of D-328. Revises D-40 in part: the reputation model and the relationship model leave, and story branches, tactical decisions, and choices as story flags stand. Revises D-242 in part: the four factions stand as the powers of region one, and none tracks reputation. PR-19 loses both value systems, and the gate of Phase 3 changes. OQ-42 and OQ-45 lose their options on standing. D-542 and D-543 set the form of a flag and of a condition on 2026-09-16, and D-544 puts both in PR-68. |
| D-330 | 2026-09-13 | The battle in the cells | The party breaks Dagvar, a hexer of the old faith, out of the hanging cells before he hangs. The church then sends Elio to learn where the hexer goes. Dagvar is a wanted man from the first hours of the game. |
| D-331 | 2026-09-13 | When the party reaches the refuge | During the flight. After the bishop dies, the party escapes through the sealed gallery to the refuge of the old faith, and then it climbs to the pass. The session recommended the road through the deep mine, and the owner chose the flight. | Applies D-248, D-251, D-313, D-319, and D-325. The second hub opens late in region one, after the reports of Elio end (D-317). The way from the refuge to the pass, and whether the hunt finds the refuge, are questions. |
| D-332 | 2026-09-13 | The hunt | Church wardens, the armed men of the foreign church, capture the party and then hunt it out of region one. The session recommended this. | Answers the questions of D-314 and D-318. Applies D-155, D-252, D-324, and D-326. Revises D-314 in part: the guild does not hunt the witnesses. The soldiers of D-318 and D-319 are church wardens. |
| D-333 | 2026-09-13 | From the refuge to the pass | The party goes back through the gallery and past the town by night, to the road up to the pass. The session recommended a hidden path of the old faith, and the owner chose the town. | Applies D-250, D-251, D-313, and D-331. The flight passes the home of Marrek one last time. The hunted party walks under the walls of the church, so the arc needs a reason for the risk. D-343 sets it: the wardens are behind the party. Revised in part by D-371 on 2026-09-13, the effect only: the mining town is not the home of Marrek. The night pass stands. |
| D-334 | 2026-09-13 | The hunt finds the refuge | The church wardens follow the party to the refuge of the old faith and raid it for sheltering the party. The church gets what it wanted through the flight of the party, and the cost weighs on Dagvar. D-131 still holds: the old faith does not fall as a faction. What the party sees of the raid is a question. |
| D-335 | 2026-09-13 | Harm to children, and aftermaths | No text or scene shows harm to a child directly. Text can imply that harm, or state it without direct detail. Scenes can show the aftermath of violence, deaths of every kind included. The owner gave this with D-334. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-13: "We don't directly show harm to any children, but it can be implied or stated without direct text. We can also show aftermaths, including deaths to all types." Revises D-126 in part: the limit on harm to a child narrows to a direct showing. The limit on sexual violence stands. Revises D-70 in part: rule 13 of the `game-text-style` skill changes. |
| D-336 | 2026-09-13 | The first fights | Marrek fights alone in the first fights of the game. New characters join the party slowly, one at a time, from there. The session recommended that Bergit and Ottild go into the cells with Marrek, and the owner chose Marrek alone. | Applies D-31, D-267, and D-280. Battle holds one, two, or three characters, so PR-9 plans for fewer than three. The first playable holds fewer characters than the options of OQ-46 assume. The order in which the others join is a question. D-342 sets it. |
| D-337 | 2026-09-13 | Why Marrek frees Dagvar | Dagvar kept rites at a waystone in the deep mine and saw the parent of Marrek go down. The session recommended this. | Applies D-141, D-291, D-295, and D-330. The jailbreak serves the search of the lead. Someone besides Bergit knows about the parent, so her secret carries less weight alone (D-294). |
| D-338 | 2026-09-13 | What Bergit knows | Bergit knows that the crew was alive behind the seal, that the collapse is a lie, and where the sealed level is. She does not know what waits below, or who was in the crew. The session recommended this. | Applies D-294, D-311, D-312, and D-326. Her secret opens the way to the sealed level, and the wrong thing shocks her too. The reason of the guild for the seal comes out only in the deep mine. |
| D-339 | 2026-09-13 | The first to join | The first character who joins Marrek gives the party its purpose to free Dagvar from the cells. The owner asked for careful thought on the order of the joins. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-13: "The first character that joins needs to give a purpose for us to get Dagvar out of the cells." Applies D-330, D-336, and D-337. Dagvar is the one freed, and Elio joins after the cells (D-316), so the first to join is Bergit or Ottild. The full order is a question. D-342 sets it. |
| D-340 | 2026-09-13 | What the party sees of the raid | Nothing on screen. The party leaves the refuge before the wardens come. On the road up to the pass, a survivor catches up with the party and tells what happened. The session recommended that the party see the raid start, and the owner chose the aftermath told. | Applies D-333, D-334, and D-335. The escape stays clean, and the cost lands as grief and guilt. The risk of the road past the town (D-333) still needs a reason, and that is a question. D-343 sets it. |
| D-341 | 2026-09-13 | The border fort | The bandits take the toll of the party, then sell the party to the church wardens for a bounty. The party fights its way out of the fort. The session recommended this. | Applies D-249, D-252, D-255, D-313, and D-332. The passes pay, and the fort earns its place as a dungeon. The fort repeats the beat of a betrayal and an escape after the cells (D-327), so the two dungeons need different shapes. |
| D-342 | 2026-09-13 | The order of the joins | Marrek fights alone first. Then Bergit joins: she needs a witness that the crew went down alive, and the hexer at the waystone saw it. Dagvar joins when the party frees him from the cells. Elio joins after the cells. Ottild joins before the deep mine, because her tunnels are the way in. The session recommended this. | Answers the question of D-339. Applies D-294, D-296, D-316, D-330, D-336, D-337, and D-338. The lie of the guild drives the first act. A former guild warden storms the cells of the church, so Bergit crosses two powers at once. The first playable holds Marrek, Bergit, and Dagvar, and Elio if it runs past the cells (OQ-46). |
| D-343 | 2026-09-13 | Why the party risks the town | The wardens track the party into the gallery toward the refuge, so the town is thinly guarded, and the road up is open for one night. The session recommended this. | Answers the questions of D-333 and D-340. Applies D-332 and D-334: the hunt that dooms the refuge opens the escape of the party, which sharpens the guilt. The gallery needs a second passage, so the party and the wardens pass each other, and the layout of the gallery sets it. |
| D-344 | 2026-09-13 | The trace of the parent | Pick marks open a crawlway out of the sealed level. The miner's mark of the parent is cut at turns that climb toward the pass. The session recommended this. | Applies D-291, D-311, and D-314, and rule 10 of the `game-text-style` skill: the player finds the trace, and nobody tells it. The old galleries reach from the deep mine toward the pass, a long way through the mountain. |
| D-345 | 2026-09-13 | The end of region one | The church wardens catch the party on the ice crossing, and the last fight of region one is against their captain in the wind. The session recommended this. | Applies D-244, D-313, and D-332. The hunt ends in the finale, and weather and ice shape the last fight. Church men are the foe in three late fights: the cells, the fort, and the ice (D-327, D-341). |
| D-346 | 2026-09-13 | The first fights | Marrek goes down through old cellars and drains under the mining town, to look for a way into the deep mine. He fights beasts and scavengers there. Bergit, who knows the old ways, finds him. The session recommended this. | Answers where the fights of D-336 happen. Applies D-155, D-291, D-338, and D-342. No power of the town is the first foe of Marrek, so the guild stays neither evil nor good (D-324). The area under the town is small and lies outside the four dungeons (D-56), and the first playable builds it with the town and the cells (PR-17). Superseded by D-370 on 2026-09-13: the first fights happen near the village of D-368. D-575 on 2026-09-16 makes the count five dungeons, with the sealed gallery. This answer stands. |
| D-347 | 2026-09-13 | Why Bergit crosses the pass | Nothing is left for her in region one. The guild took her mark, the church hunts her for the jailbreak, and she has no place left in the region. The session recommended the proof of the sealed crew, and the owner chose loss. | Applies D-294, D-315, D-330, and D-332. Her reason is plain and grim. Loss alone gives her little drive in region two, so region two needs to give her more. |
| D-348 | 2026-09-13 | Why Dagvar crosses the pass | The old faith says that its founders raised the waystones, and Dagvar goes to find stones beyond the reach of the church. The session recommended this. | Applies D-141, D-156, D-295, and D-315. His rites carry his story into region two. The mystery of the stones pays off only at the end of the game (D-131), so region two gives him little resolution. |
| D-349 | 2026-09-13 | Why Ottild crosses the pass | Loyalty she did not expect. Ottild came for the blade of Marrek and a fee, and after the mine, the cells, and the refuge, she stays for the party. The session recommended a bounty on her head, and the owner chose loyalty. | Answers the second reason that D-296 asks for. Applies D-296 and D-315. The arc of a cynic who finds people worth staying for is a familiar turn for a thief, so her scenes need a shape of their own. |
| D-350 | 2026-09-13 | Choices in region one | A few set choices: two or three at set points in region one. Each choice is a story flag that a later scene or region reads. The session recommended this. | Applies D-40, D-163, D-267, and D-329. The player shapes some of what follows, and each choice stays a simple flag (PR-18). Every choice needs text for each result, and the save of the prologue carries each flag. Which choices is the next question. D-354 sets one: the captain on the ice. |
| D-351 | 2026-09-13 | Scenes that set the fighters (OQ-49) | No. The player always picks the three who fight, and no scene or boss sets who fights. The session recommended this. | Resolves OQ-49. Applies D-61, D-62, D-73, and D-292. No scene forces a weak character into a fight alone, so no run sticks on one difficulty and one save slot. The story cannot split the party for a fight. The first fights of Marrek alone force no pick, because nobody else has joined (D-336). |
| D-352 | 2026-09-13 | When the personal tasks get their content | In the arc content PRs. PR-28 and PR-29 propose each personal task of region one, and the owner approves the batch. The session recommended one line per task in this block. | Applies D-57, D-282, and D-287. The arc file leaves five tasks open, and the systems block plans the tasks of PR-19 without their content. |
| D-353 | 2026-09-13 | The two months after region one | Region one ends on the far side of the ice. The arc of region two sets what the two months change. The save carries the party and the flags as they stand. The session recommended this. | Applies D-163, D-253, D-254, and D-345. The prologue ends clean, and region two keeps its freedom. The open item of `docs/world/places.md` on the two months moves to region two. |
| D-354 | 2026-09-13 | The choice on the ice | After the last fight of region one, the party spares the beaten captain of the wardens, or kills him. Region two reads the flag. The owner picked this one of four proposed choices. The session recommended it, together with the other prisoners in the cells. | Applies D-329, D-345, and D-350. The prologue ends on a choice between mercy and blood. A spared captain must return in a later region, or the choice feels empty. D-350 asks for two or three choices and the owner picked one, so a question asks how many. D-355 settles it: the content PRs propose the rest. |
| D-355 | 2026-09-13 | How many set choices | The choice on the ice is set now. PR-28 and PR-29 propose one or two more set choices for region one, and the owner approves them, as with the personal tasks. The session recommended this. | Answers the question of D-354. D-350 stands: region one holds two or three set choices. Applies D-57 and D-352. The roadmaps plan the flags of region one without knowing all of them. |

## Full plan: systems area

The systems block of the full plan (D-142, D-262) started on 2026-09-13, after the arc block. Each answer is one row.

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-356 | 2026-09-13 | Where lessons sit (OQ-38) | Each character has lesson slots, and the slot count grows with the character level. The player swaps lessons at a hub or at a save point. The session recommended swaps at hubs alone, and the owner chose hubs and save points. | Resolves OQ-38, and answers C-9 of the second critic pass (F-28). Applies D-58, D-257, and D-274. At a save point, a fresh reserve character can take the rites of a spent caster, so more MP reaches the fights of a dungeon, and the balance of D-35 must hold with it (M-4). After the breakout, the refuge and the save points of the fort and the ice are the places to swap (D-331). Revised in part by D-1050 on 2026-09-24: the place of the swap. The player swaps lessons anywhere outside a fight. The slots and the growth of the slot count stand. |
| D-357 | 2026-09-13 | How a lesson grows (OQ-39) | Every equipped lesson gains points from each battle won, used or not, and it unlocks stronger versions at set totals. The lessons of a reserve character gain half. The session recommended this. | Resolves OQ-39, and answers the open part of D-34. Applies D-45, D-73, and D-272: no lesson copies itself, so the loot table stays finite. Each lesson has a second progress track, which needs a display and balance work. D-361 sets whose growth it is: the character's. D-539 sets what a stronger version is on 2026-09-16: a named form with the point total that opens it. Revised in part by D-1022 on 2026-09-24: a lesson of a downed character gains no points. The rest stands. D-1019 sets the points of a battle. |
| D-358 | 2026-09-13 | What an aptitude does (OQ-40) | A lesson of the kind of a character's aptitude works better, by a bonus in basis points, such as more damage or more healing. The session recommended this. | Resolves OQ-40. Applies D-169, D-274, and D-281. Any character uses any lesson, and the right character uses it best. The size of the bonus of a side aptitude is the next question. D-360 sets it: half. |
| D-359 | 2026-09-13 | An action with no MP (OQ-44) | Every character can attack with the weapon in hand, and no lesson gives that attack. The session recommended this. | Resolves OQ-44, and keeps F-8. Applies D-35, D-42, and D-336: a caster with empty MP still acts, and Marrek has a move while he fights alone. Binds PR-9. |
| D-360 | 2026-09-13 | The bonus of a side aptitude | A side aptitude gives half the bonus of the main aptitude. The session recommended this. | Applies D-282 and D-358. The main aptitude stays what a character does best, and a missed task costs less. A whole personal task earns a smaller reward. |
| D-361 | 2026-09-13 | Whose growth a lesson carries | Growth belongs to the character, not to the lesson. Each character keeps their own points and unlocked versions for every lesson they have carried. A lesson passed to a character who never carried it starts at its first level for that character. When it comes back, it resumes where that character left it. The session recommended that the points belong to the lesson, and the owner gave this answer. | Owner answer, 2026-09-13: "Per character, but lesson-unlocks STAY as a character trait, not a lesson-item-specific trait." Refines D-357: the points of a lesson count per character. Progress belongs to the character and the lesson, not to one copy of it. The menu and the save track progress per character per lesson (D-166). |
| D-362 | 2026-09-13 | The first playable (OQ-46) | The first playable holds Marrek, Bergit, and Dagvar, with more lessons. The build ends when Dagvar joins in the hanging cells. The session recommended that the build run until Elio joins, and the owner chose the end of the cells. | Resolves OQ-46. Applies D-51, D-313, D-336, D-342, and D-346. Nobody waits in reserve at Gate 2, so the play of the party swap and the lesson swap at a save point moves to a later gate. PR-14 and PR-16 still test both with a fixture party. D-369 and D-370 add the village and its first fights to the build. |
| D-363 | 2026-09-13 | The level of a newcomer (OQ-50) | Each character who joins has a set level in content. The session recommended the average level of the characters who have joined, and the owner chose a set level. | Resolves OQ-50, and the rest of C-12 of the second critic pass, except OQ-51 (F-28). Applies D-270, D-299, and D-342. A player far above or below that level meets a weak or a strong newcomer, and the replacement of Elio must still cover what Elio did (D-321). The bots test each join level (D-64). D-388 keeps the party near those levels with a soft cap. |
| D-364 | 2026-09-13 | The lessons and gear of Elio (OQ-41) | They go with him. When Elio dies, the lessons and the gear that he carries leave the game. The session recommended that they pass to the party, and the owner chose the loss. | Resolves OQ-41. Applies D-27, D-270, D-321, and D-361. The death costs everything Elio carried, and a player who loaded Elio heavily loses a large share of the build in one scene. D-270 still holds: the replacement must cover what Elio did, so later content must offer lessons to take the place of his. D-1025 on 2026-09-24 confirms this decision, and a shop after his death sells one copy of each lesson that left with him. |
| D-365 | 2026-09-13 | Where lessons come from (OQ-47) | Three sources: dungeons hold lessons as treasure, hub shops sell some, and people in the story teach or give others, such as the old-faith hexer and the fence at the refuge. The session recommended this. | Resolves OQ-47. Applies D-41, D-45, D-60, D-246, and D-304. Every place and faction gives something. A shop that sells a lesson lets the party own two copies, so the content plans for copies, and progress stays with the character (D-361). In region one, the shops of the town close to the party at the breakout, and the refuge is open only in the flight (D-319, D-331). Revised in part by D-1023 on 2026-09-24: the player never owns two copies of one lesson, so the line on two copies leaves. The three sources stand. |
| D-366 | 2026-09-13 | Licenses and stamps in play (OQ-42) | Story only. The party never gets a license or a stamp. Only Elio casts legally, and every other rite in the party breaks the law from the start. The session recommended this at the ask, after the arc made the party outlaws. | Resolves OQ-42. Applies D-130, D-302, D-308, D-319, D-328, and D-330. The law bites as the story does, and no system sells licenses or stamps. The law gives gold no use, and stamps matter in scenes alone. |
| D-367 | 2026-09-13 | Unlawful rites in play (OQ-45) | The story alone. Scenes and dialogue carry the risk of an unlawful rite, and no rule in play tracks it. The session recommended this. | Resolves OQ-45. Applies D-302, D-318, D-329, D-332, and D-366. No new system enters Core, and a rite cast in a hub has no cost in play. |
| D-368 | 2026-09-13 | Where Marrek grew up | Marrek grew up in a small village near the mining town, and his parent walked from there to work the deep mine. The game starts in the village. The owner gave the start in the village, and the session recommended that the village be his home. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-13: "I want the starting character NOT to start in the mine town, but to start in a small village near the mine town." Revises D-284 in part: the lead grew up in the village, not in the mining town. He stays a young local commoner under the ban, and the politics of the town reach him second-hand at first. |
| D-369 | 2026-09-13 | What the village is | A small start area: a few houses to walk through, with scenes and people, and no shop or rest service. The session recommended this. | Applies D-28, D-56, and D-368: region one keeps two hubs and four dungeons. The opening stays short, and the first playable builds the village (D-362). D-575 on 2026-09-16 makes the count five dungeons, with the sealed gallery. This answer stands. |
| D-370 | 2026-09-13 | The first fights, again | Marrek fights alone near the village, and Bergit finds him there. The session recommended that the fights stay under the town, and the owner chose the village. | Supersedes D-346: the cellars and drains under the town lose their role, and the first playable builds the land near the village in their place. Applies D-336 and D-342. Bergit needs a reason to be near the village, and that is a question. D-372 sets it: she guards the road in. |
| D-371 | 2026-09-13 | Where the village sits | On the road in: below the high valley, on the road up from the foothills, a short walk from the mining town. The supply road of the occupier passes the village. The session recommended this. | Revises D-250 in part: the layout gains the village on the road in. Applies D-254 and D-369. The player climbs into the region with Marrek, and the flight at the end climbs away from the village, so region one never returns to it. The mining town is not the home of Marrek (D-333, D-368). |
| D-372 | 2026-09-13 | Why Bergit is near the village | With no guild mark, Bergit hires out her blade to guard ore carts and travelers on the road in. The fight near the village falls on her route. The session recommended this. | Answers the question of D-370. Applies D-249, D-294, D-338, D-342, and D-371. A warden without work does the work of a warden for coin, and D-338 stands. The meeting is chance. When Marrek says that his parent vanished below, Bergit comes close to a guess about who was in the crew. |
| D-373 | 2026-09-13 | The first foes | Hungry beasts come down from the high ground to the village through the deep snow of mid-winter. The session recommended this. | Applies D-155, D-253, and D-370. No power of the region is the first foe of Marrek. The opening fights carry no story weight beyond survival. |
| D-374 | 2026-09-13 | Limits on gear (OQ-43) | Nothing limits gear. Any character wears any gear, as any character equips any lesson. The session recommended this. | Resolves OQ-43, and answers the open part of D-44. Applies D-274 and D-358: the aptitudes carry the difference between characters. Gear gives no character a shape of its own. |
| D-375 | 2026-09-13 | When a missed task closes (OQ-51) | Each region closes its own personal tasks when the region ends. The session recommended a set story point with a notice, and the owner chose the end of the region. | Resolves OQ-51, and the last part of C-12 of the second critic pass (F-28). Applies D-163, D-221, and D-282. A task in the mining town becomes impossible at the breakout, with no notice until region one ends (D-319). The save carries the result into region two. |
| D-376 | 2026-09-13 | The turn order | Action delay. Each action pushes its user back on the timeline by an amount that the action and the speed of the user set. The strip shows the next several turns. A heavy blow pushes further, haste shortens every push, and a stun can push an enemy back. The session recommended this. | Applies D-29, D-35, D-164, and T-7: every choice moves the order, and the delays count ticks. A fast character can act twice before a slow one, so balance needs care (M-4). Binds PR-9 and PR-10. |
| D-377 | 2026-09-13 | Rows in battle | Each side has a front row and a back row. Melee reaches only the front row while anyone stands in it. Shot drills and rites reach either row. The session recommended this. | Applies D-35, D-111, D-281, and D-336. Guard, Shot, and Blade each gain a role, and enemy formations become real. Placement becomes a choice in every fight, with rules and bot tests (D-64). Binds PR-9, PR-10, and PR-11. |
| D-378 | 2026-09-13 | Fleeing a battle | Any character can try to flee on their turn. The chance rises with the speed of the party, a failed try costs the turn, and no party can flee from a boss. The session recommended this. | Applies D-35, D-231, and G-4. Retreat is always possible and never free. The chance draws on a seeded random stream, with tests. Binds PR-9. |
| D-379 | 2026-09-13 | The feeding as a battle rule (OQ-35) | No rule. The feeding of the thing below stays a story fact. The session recommended this. | Resolves OQ-35. Applies D-42, D-135, and D-148. No new system enters Core, and every spell stays a budget choice. A player never feels the cost in play. |
| D-380 | 2026-09-13 | Row changes in battle | A character can step to the other row on their turn, and the step pushes the character back on the timeline like a light action. The session recommended this. | Applies D-65, D-376, and D-377. A hurt fighter can fall back, and a caster can step up. Enemies take the same move, and the evaluator scores it. Binds PR-9 and PR-11. |
| D-381 | 2026-09-13 | An enemy group after a flee | The group stays on the map and returns to its route. No battle with that group starts for a short grace time, from either side, while the party moves away. The session recommended this. | Applies D-37, D-164, D-265, D-378, and L-9. A flee cannot set up an attack from behind at once. The grace time counts ticks and needs its own tests. Binds PR-8. |
| D-382 | 2026-09-13 | Items in battle and in the pack | Any character can use an item on their turn, and the use counts as an action on the timeline. The pack holds a small, fixed number of each item. An item restores less in battle than outside battle. The owner added the smaller effect in battle to the recommended option. | Applies D-35, D-36, D-42, D-45, and D-376. Items stay scarce, and every character can reach them. The size of the reduction is a balance number (PR-30). A find over the stack limit needs a rule, and that is a question. D-45 says that consumables were not chosen, so a question asks which items get used up. D-384 and D-385 settle both. Binds PR-9 and PR-13. |
| D-383 | 2026-09-13 | What a steal takes | Each enemy carries a short, hand-authored list of items and some gold. A successful steal takes one entry. A human enemy carries what a person would. The session recommended this. | Applies D-45, D-155, D-281, and G-21. Theft pays in every fight with people, and the list stays authored. Every enemy profile needs a steal list, which grows the content. Binds PR-11. |
| D-384 | 2026-09-13 | Which items get used up | A small, hand-placed set of items gets used up: MP draughts, healing, cures for statuses, and the rare revive. The session recommended this. | Answers the conflict of D-45 against D-36, D-42, and D-382. Revises D-45 in part: the line "Consumables were not chosen" leaves. Fixed hand-authored items, rarity tiers, and no random affixes or crafting stand. Applies D-35: the set stays scarce, and it needs writing and balance. |
| D-385 | 2026-09-13 | A find over the stack limit | The extra stays in the chest or on the spot, and the party can come back for it. The session recommended this. | Applies D-45, D-166, and D-382. Nothing hand-placed is lost. A chest can be partly emptied, so the save records what remains. Binds PR-13 and PR-16. |
| D-386 | 2026-09-13 | Theft on the map | The layout marks some locks as pickable, and a Theft drill carried by one of the three who fight opens them. A story lock always needs its key. A Theft drill also reveals and disarms traps. The session recommended this. | Applies D-41, D-165, and D-281. Theft gains a role on the map, and the author decides which locks it opens. Every lock and trap needs a mark in the layout. Binds PR-7 and PR-16. |
| D-387 | 2026-09-13 | Experience for a downed character | A downed character earns half experience from a battle won, as a character in reserve does. The session recommended this. | Applies D-36 and D-73. One rule covers anyone who did not fight, and a down does not snowball. A down costs little experience. Binds PR-12. Superseded by D-974 on 2026-09-23: a downed character earns no experience. |
| D-388 | 2026-09-13 | The level range | A soft cap per region: experience from an enemy shrinks as the party outlevels it. The session recommended this. | Applies D-37, D-163, D-257, and D-363. Fixed join levels stay fair, and region two starts in a known range. A player who grinds sees the reward fade, and the curve needs tuning (PR-30). Binds PR-12. |
| D-389 | 2026-09-13 | What a save point restores | MP alone, once per visit. A save point restores no health. The session recommended this. | Applies D-35, D-198, D-257, and D-384. Health comes back at hubs, from items, and from Mend rites, so health stays the scarce resource inside a dungeon. A party with no Mend rite and few items limps to the end of a dungeon. Binds PR-16. D-442 supersedes D-198 on 2026-09-14, and the restore rule stands. Revised in part by D-555 on 2026-09-16: the MP restore runs once for the place, not once for each visit. MP alone, and no health, stand. |
| D-390 | 2026-09-13 | Statuses after a battle | Poison, blind, and silence last past the end of a battle, until a cure or a rest at a hub. Poison ticks on the map. Every other status ends with the battle, and a down stays (D-36). The session recommended that every status end with the battle, and the owner chose that some last. | Applies D-35, D-75, D-212, and D-384. Cure items and Mend matter between fights, and a dungeon wears the party down. The map needs rules for these statuses, with tests and a mark on the HUD. What poison, blind, and silence do outside battle are questions. D-392 and D-393 set them. Binds PR-16. |
| D-391 | 2026-09-13 | Rites outside battle | Mend rites, and rites that cure afflictions, work from the menu outside battle, at their MP cost. Every other rite stays in battle. The session recommended Mend alone, and the owner added the cures. | Owner answer, 2026-09-13: "Mend + affliction cure". Applies D-42, D-389, and D-390. MP stays the budget for healing and cures between fights, and the menu needs a cast screen with target rules. The kind of a rite that cures an affliction is a question (D-281). D-394 sets it: Mend. Binds PR-12. |
| D-392 | 2026-09-13 | Poison on the map | Poison on the map can down a character, and that character stays down until a hub or a rare item. The session recommended that poison stop at 1 health, and the owner chose the down. | Applies D-27, D-36, D-225, and D-390. The wear-down is harsh, and a party can lose fighters while it walks. What happens when poison downs all three who fight is a question. D-395 set a wipe, D-396 replaced it with the reserve, and D-397 restored the wipe. Binds PR-16. |
| D-393 | 2026-09-13 | Blind and silence outside battle | Outside battle, blind does nothing, and silence stops any rite cast from the menu. Both apply in the next battle until a cure. ## One PR, one session

The owner set the session rule on 2026-09-16, to keep the context of each session small, and answered three questions of PR #14. The owner corrected the end point of a session during the review of PR #14. Each answer is one row.

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-576 | 2026-09-16 | One PR in each session | Each PR starts in a new, clean, top-level session. A session binds to one repository, one branch, one PR, and one role: author, reviewer, or correction author. A session never works on a second PR. Many clean sessions can work on one PR. A fork, a subagent, a compaction, or a summary of a session that worked on another PR is not clean. The session ends at the hand-over point of its PR. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-16. Revises D-18 in part: the session model. The `one-pr-one-session` skill holds the gates, and `CLAUDE.md` requires it for all PR work. The harness gives no session id, so the owner and the session enforce the binding, and no check can. Revised in part by D-582 on 2026-09-16, the end point only: the author session stays bound through each review of its PR. The clean start, the binding, and one PR in each session stand. Revised in part by D-583 on 2026-09-16, start gate line 2 only: a session reads the start set, not the whole read order. The rest stands. Revised in part by D-926 on 2026-09-23: the author session starts the reviewer as a separate Codex process, which is a clean reviewer session of its own. The binding and one PR in each session stand. |
| D-577 | 2026-09-16 | The PR is the complete unit | A PR holds its change, its tests, its decisions, its questions, its documents, its review records, and its handoff entries. Every document that the change affects changes in the same PR. The Documents section of the PR description has one line for each required document or category, with a specific reason for each document that does not change. No PR defers a document to a later PR. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-16. Replaces the PR gate line "no change needed because ..." with the Documents section of the skill. Applies T-5 and D-18. |
| D-578 | 2026-09-16 | No PR for a merge record | Before the merge, the records give the state of the PR, its effective head, the checks, the review result, and that the PR waits for the owner merge. Git and GitHub hold the merge commit and the merge time. No PR exists only to record the merge, the handoff, the review record, or the documents of an earlier PR. The next PR reads its base from git. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-16. Revises D-18 in part together with D-576. The effective head rule of the `pr-review` skill stands: a metadata commit never moves it. The cross-provider review stands (T-4). Revised in part by D-930 on 2026-09-23, the words of the wait alone: the records say that the PR waits for the auto-merge, or for the owner merge under D-931. The rest stands. |
| D-579 | 2026-09-16 | Where the document rules run | PR-3 adds the document rules to the `review-gate` command. The command fails a PR whose diff does not change `docs/session-handoff.md`, whose Documents section lacks a required row or has an empty line, or whose description defers a document or a record of the PR itself to a later PR. A line that names the PR of independent roadmap work passes (G-16). Until PR-3 merges, the session, the reviewer, and the owner enforce the rules. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-16. Revises D-15 in part: the scope of the command. The rules stay in C# with xUnit fixtures (D-99, D-101, T-3). The workflow runs again when the PR description changes. |
| D-580 | 2026-09-16 | A docs PR with its own concern | A docs PR whose concern is new work, such as an interview, a critic pass, or a runbook, stays a PR of its own, with its own Documents section and handoff entry. A PR that only records the merge, the handoff, the review, or the documents of an earlier PR breaks D-577 and D-578. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-16. Applies D-68 and D-401 without change. |
| D-581 | 2026-09-16 | The forms of a Documents line | Each line has one of three forms: `Changed: <path>. <reason>`, `No change needed because <reason that names the path>`, or `Not applicable because <specific reason>`. The session recommended these, which pass the STE check. | Owner answer, 2026-09-16. The other repositories of the owner use a form with a semicolon, which breaks STE rule 8.1 (D-10). |
| D-582 | 2026-09-16 | A review answer stays in the session | The author session answers each gitar comment and each review of its PR. A review answer needs no new session. The author session ends when a review record gives `Ready for owner merge` for the effective head, or when the label is on. A new session from the provider of the author takes the author role only when the author session no longer exists. The reviewer session repeats its review of the same PR after each correction, and it ends at the same verdict or when the owner ends the review. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-16, after the session blocked the answer to the review of PR #14: "Addressing Codex/gitar review feedback does NOT qualify for a new session." Revises D-576 in part: the end point of the author session. It narrows the owner instruction of 2026-09-16 that a session ends at the handoff boundary of its PR. Revised in part by D-930 on 2026-09-23, the end point alone: the author session ends after the merge and the transitional prompt. The rest stands. |

## Session context budget

A token audit on 2026-09-16 made the recommendations. The owner approved them with the words "Implement all fixes as recommended." The audit read the usage records of 10 Claude Code sessions and 10 Codex sessions. `docs/runbooks/session-context.md` holds the evidence and the commands.

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-583 | 2026-09-16 | The start set and targeted reads | Each session reads a start set in full: `CLAUDE.md` or `AGENTS.md`, the top handoff entry, and the skills of the task. It reads each other document of the read order with a targeted read by id or heading, and it cites each id that the work touches. A critic pass reads whole files in the `design-critic` agent. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-16. Revises D-576 in part: start gate line 2 of the `one-pr-one-session` skill. The read order stays as the index. Whole reads of the read order held about 29% of the carried context. |
| D-584 | 2026-09-16 | The top handoff entry | At the start, a session reads the top entry of `docs/session-handoff.md` alone. It reads an older entry only when the top entry points to it, or when a gate needs the entries of the PR, such as the provider gate of a review. At the end, it reads the highest session number with one command. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-16. D-18 stands: the file keeps 10 entries with six parts. The full file is about 12k tokens, and the top entry is about 1.3k. |
| D-585 | 2026-09-16 | The STE check in the commit command | The session runs the STE checker on the staged `.md` files in the same command as `git diff --check` and the commit. A failed check stops the commit and prints the last 20 lines. The full check runs one time before the first push of a PR. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-16. D-10 and G-12 stand. A separate call for each check cost 19% of the input tokens. Revised in part by D-608 on 2026-09-17: the checker reads the whole checkout, and not the staged files alone. The rest stands. |
| D-586 | 2026-09-16 | One Gitar wait | The session waits for Gitar with one command. The command stops when a Gitar comment has an edit time after the recorded time, or after five minutes. The runbook holds the command. The shared `gitar-review` skill stays the same in each repo, and the rule of this repo adds to it. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-16. D-14 stands. A separate call for each poll cost 14% of the input tokens. Revised in part by D-895 on 2026-09-22, for the time of the gitar pause: no session runs the Gitar wait. The command stays in the runbook. D-909 ended the pause on 2026-09-23. Revised in part by D-945 on 2026-09-23, for the time of the Gitar pause: no session runs the Gitar wait. The command stays in the runbook. D-1073 ended the Gitar pause on 2026-09-24. Revised in part by D-1074 on 2026-09-24: after a push, the Gitar poll is the one wait. The command of this row stays for the wait after a `Gitar review` comment. |
| D-587 | 2026-09-16 | Context compaction at a wait | The session commits the handoff entry of a round before the push of that round, and it does not push while the PR waits for gitar. While the PR waits for gitar or the other provider, the session tells the owner that the session is ready for a context compaction. The session does the same when its context passes 300k tokens. After the context compaction, it reads the top handoff entry again. A context compaction of the session keeps the session and its binding. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-16. Applies D-576 and D-582 without change: the session stays bound, and the author answers each review. The harness gives the compaction command to the owner alone. Revised in part by D-895 on 2026-09-22, for the time of the gitar pause: no PR waits for gitar. The commit, push, and context compaction rules stand for the wait for the other provider. D-909 ended the pause on 2026-09-23. |
| D-588 | 2026-09-16 | The split of the `pr-review` skill | `.claude/skills/pr-review/SKILL.md` keeps the rules of every review, at most 18,000 bytes. Reference files hold the rules of one case each: the project contracts, the repeat review, the review record, the author answer, and the commit and session end. The core names the case that loads each file. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-16. T-4, D-17, and the machine-read parts of the review record stand. The skill was 35,449 bytes. |
| D-589 | 2026-09-16 | Review evidence in stages | The reviewer reads `git diff --stat` of the whole PR, then the diff of each path, and lists each path as inspected or uninspected. A repeat review reads the whole stat and the diff since the reviewed head. The reviewer saves the PR comments to one file with one command. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-16. The complete scope of a review stands (T-4). |
| D-590 | 2026-09-16 | The glossary reference file | `ste-writing` keeps the process terms. `references/glossary.md` holds the terms of the game, the world, the art, the audio, the effects, the UI, the story, the tools, CI, and the release. A session loads it before it writes or reviews text about those areas. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-16. D-12 stands: one term per concept. The checker does not read the glossary, so the load rule carries the risk. |
| D-591 | 2026-09-16 | Scripts and edits | The session saves a script of more than 10 lines to a file one time and runs the file again. It edits the section that changes, and it writes a whole file only when the file is new. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-16. The model output that the sessions carried held about 48% of the context in the audit estimate. |

## The Deck test and PR-1

The owner answered these questions on 2026-09-16, in the Deck test session of step 7.1 of `docs/roadmaps/phase-1-foundations.md`. That step has no PR and no review, so PR-1 records each answer here. The branch `spike/deck-test` holds the test scene and the handover file with the answers.

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-592 | 2026-09-16 | The test runner mode (OQ-75) | `dotnet test` runs in Microsoft.Testing.Platform mode (MTP), and `xunit.v3` is the one test package. `global.json` sets the test runner. The test command takes `--solution`, and it excludes the smoke tests with `--filter-not-trait`. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-16. Resolves OQ-75. ## The STE checker in C#

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-604 | 2026-09-17 | The rule for a numbered item | The 20-word limit of rule 5.1 reads every numbered list item, under any heading. A bullet item and a paragraph sentence take the 25-word limit of rule 6.3. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-17. Answers OQ-67 and closes F-5. The interim checker already applied this rule, so no document changes. The `ste-writing` skill states the one rule. |
| D-605 | 2026-09-17 | What the reference check covers | The reference check of the `ste-check` command fails three things in a live document. REF 1: an id that no register holds, for the prefixes D, OQ, F, G, T, L, M, and PR. REF 2: a path of this repository in backticks that no file or folder holds. REF 3: a citation of a superseded decision that names no decision which superseded it. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-17. Answers OQ-68. The register of each prefix is one file: `docs/decisions.md`, `docs/questions.md`, `docs/design.md`, and, for a PR, section 8 of `docs/design.md` with the PR headings of the phase files. A path counts as a path of this repository when its first part names a top-level folder of the repository, or when it is a bare file name with a file type of the repository. A line that names a `PR-#` marks each path of that PR (G-16). Revised in part by D-606 on 2026-09-17: REF 3 reads every live document except `docs/decisions.md`. |
| D-606 | 2026-09-17 | REF 3 and the decision register | The superseded-citation rule reads every live document except `docs/decisions.md`. The Effect column of that register records each supersession, and a row of it is a record of one day. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-17. Revises D-605 in part, and REF 1 and REF 2 still read the register. 28 historical rows keep their text. A row that cites a superseded decision with no successor stays a matter for the review. |
| D-607 | 2026-09-17 | The handoff entry count | The session number check fails a handoff that holds more than 10 entries, and it names the archive. It also fails a repeated session number and an entry out of order. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-17. Gives D-18 and L-12 a machine check. The handoff held 11 entries on 2026-09-17, and PR-2 moved Session 65 and Session 64 to the archive. |
| D-608 | 2026-09-17 | The checker reads the whole checkout | The `ste-check` command reads every live document of the checkout, and it takes no file list. The commit command runs it one time, with no `git diff --cached` pipeline. A full run takes about one second. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-17. Revises D-585 in part: the checker no longer reads the staged files alone, and the rest of D-585 stands. The reference check resolves each id and each path against the whole checkout, so a staged change can break a citation in a file that the commit does not touch. `docs/runbooks/session-context.md` holds the new command. Revised in part by D-702 on 2026-09-20: the command reads the tracked files alone, and it still takes no file list. |

The number is the GitHub number of the pull request. A commit that changes the record of another pull request moves the effective head. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-17. Answers OQ-181, which blocked PR-3. Revises D-603 in part, the paths of the set alone: the folder `docs/reviews/` no longer joins the set as a whole. The rest of D-603 stands. The `ste-check` command reads every live document and runs in `make verify`, so a session sees a fault before the push. Applies D-15 and D-17. Each of the 16 records of `docs/reviews/` passes the new rule with no change to its text. A check of the prose of the section stays out of scope, because the reference file asks the reviewer to give the reason of the verdict in that prose. The `pr-review` reference file holds the rule. |

The text rule fails each member of a Godot type whose name holds `Text` or `Title`, and each member of a committed short list, such as `DrawString`. A lookup by key stays legal, so Core keeps its fast lookups. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-17. Resolves OQ-71, which blocked PR-46. Applies G-4, T-7, and F-39. A ban of both types in Core was the other option, and it makes each lookup cost more time. The lint reads one method at a time, so a walk inside a method that takes the collection as a parameter stays a matter for the review. |

## The Deck test result and the look

The owner ran the Deck test of D-160 on 2026-09-17 and answered these questions in the same session. PR-85 records each row.

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-616 | 2026-09-17 | The renderer of the Deck test (D-160) | The game uses the Mobile renderer, and PR-82 writes it into `TheThingBelow.Game/project.godot`. The Deck test measured both renderers on an OLED Deck at the frame of 1280 by 720. Mobile gave a lower frame time in each of the 20 stages. At the full load, Mobile gave 2.78 ms at the 95th percentile, and Forward+ gave 3.33 ms. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-17. Completes the process of D-160, and applies D-161 and D-599. One project setting covers each shipped platform, because the mobile and web overrides of Godot serve platforms that D-481 and D-482 exclude. The driver fallback of Godot can put a player on the Compatibility renderer, and that path is the same under each renderer. The Mobile renderer has no normal and roughness buffer, which 3D alone reads. The screen tests of CI keep the Compatibility renderer (D-172). |
| D-617 | 2026-09-17 | The first effect budget (D-523) | The effect budget takes the largest load of the sweep: 15 lights with shadows, 8192 live particles, and 3 full-screen passes. Each row is a floor, and not the limit of the Deck, because no stage of the sweep missed the target (F-66). PR-56 to PR-60 write the rows into the budget file. A later sweep with a heavier load can raise a row, with a measurement before it and after it (G-14). The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-17. Applies D-161, D-523, and F-46: Godot drops each light past 15 on one canvas item, so the light row keeps that limit. The worst stage of the sweep holds 15 lights, 8192 particles, and a transition. It gave 4.55 ms at the 95th percentile under Mobile, which is 27 percent of the frame budget. Forward+ gave 5.00 ms in the same stage. The planned load of the game sits far below each row. D-849 adds three full-screen passes in PR-92 on 2026-09-21, with a new sweep before its merge. Revised in part by D-854 on 2026-09-21: the light row rises to 24. Revised in part by D-923 on 2026-09-23: the pass row rises to 6 after the sweep of D-922. The rows of lights and particles stand. |
| D-618 | 2026-09-17 | The CRT leaves the plan | The game has no CRT effect. The curvature, the bleed, the flicker, and the scanlines leave the plan, and the toggle leaves the display group. PR-37 leaves the roadmap. The owner chose the removal. | Owner instruction, 2026-09-17. Supersedes D-88, D-105, D-120, and D-240. Revises D-210 in part: no pass covers the UI. Revises D-226 in part: the display group holds the window mode and the scale alone. Closes F-18, and F-23 loses the two screenshots of the toggle. G-19 loses the words of the CRT. OQ-100 loses the flicker, and OQ-103 loses the CRT as a user of shader code. D-619 keeps the two transitions with a CRT name. The screen tests of PR-41 lose the capture of the toggle (D-172). |
| D-619 | 2026-09-17 | The transitions with a CRT name | The transition library keeps the CRT power-off and the scanline sweep. D-194 and D-195 stand, and the library holds ten transitions. A transition is one effect between two scenes, and no pass covers the frame after D-618. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-17. Applies D-191 and D-195. The two names come from an old monitor, and a later PR can rename them. PR-60 builds the library. |
| D-620 | 2026-09-17 | The Sprite Fusion test | A spike compares the pixel art of a session with the pixel art of the Sprite Fusion generator, before PR-34. The branch spike/sprite-fusion holds the spike, and that branch never merges. The owner buys one month of the Starter plan. The spike draws the same subjects both ways, and the owner picks the source of the art. The owner chose a spike, and the session recommended a step inside PR-34. | Owner answer, 2026-09-17. Applies D-107, D-181, D-597, and G-24. External facts, read 2026-09-17: the free browser tool of Sprite Fusion is a tilemap editor, and it takes a tile set from the user. The pixel art generator is a separate paid product with a credit plan, at 9, 19, or 49 USD each month. It writes a sprite of 16, 32, or 64 pixels from a text prompt. Sources: `https://www.spritefusion.com/` and `https://www.spritefusion.com/pixel-art-generator`. The owner picks the scale of the world and the scale of the UI. The session recommended the probe, and the owner added the two desktop screens. | Owner answer, 2026-09-17. Raises OQ-183, and the probe answers it. M-8 records the result. Revises D-568 and D-228 in process: the probe decides the scale of the frame on a screen. The 32-pixel tile, the 16-pixel font, and the frame of 1280 by 720 stand until the answer. D-633 to D-640 on 2026-09-18 hold the answer, and OQ-183 closes with them. Numbers of 2026-09-17: a 32-pixel sprite covers 4.0 mm on an OLED Deck, and about 30 arcminutes at 45 cm. A 16-pixel sprite of a Game Boy Advance covers 4.09 mm, and about 50 arcminutes at 28 cm. The option that D-228 refused holds 40 tiles across the frame, so D-228 never set the scale on the screen. A move to 2x gives 20 by 11.25 tiles, and D-37 gives each screen the view of the Deck.
