| D-956 | 2026-09-23 | Where an enemy profile lives | One file for each profile, under `content/rules/profiles/`, as the enemy records do (D-786). Each entry of a group names a profile id, and an absent id fails the load. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Applies D-65, D-535, D-786, and T-2. One profile serves many groups, and PR-30 tunes each profile in one place. The weights inline in each entry of a group, and one profile on each enemy record, were the other options. |
| D-957 | 2026-09-23 | How a map knows its region | Each map file gains a `region` field. The groups move to one file for each region, under `content/rules/groups/`, and the fixture groups move to the file of a fixture region with the same ids. The load fails a map whose group is absent from the file of its region, and the error names the map and the id. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Applies D-528, D-535, D-766, and T-1. A group never leaks across regions, and the map schema changes. A folder of maps for each region was the other option. |
| D-958 | 2026-09-23 | The traits of a profile | PR-11 ships the weights of the score terms alone, with no trait. The first trait lands with the first enemy that needs one, in PR-17 or PR-20. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Applies D-65 and T-1: two concrete cases before an abstraction. The weights already make a brute and a healer act unlike each other. A small set of traits in PR-11 was the other option. |
| D-959 | 2026-09-23 | How the evaluator treats chance | The evaluator scores each action by its expected outcome, in integer math. The hit chance multiplies the damage at the middle hit factor, and a miss adds nothing. The evaluator draws no roll, except on a tie (D-947). The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Applies D-534, D-947, G-4, and T-7. One state gives one score, the cost stays low, and the battle stream never moves for a score. A gamble scores at its expected value alone. A roll on a copy of the battle was the other option. |
| D-960 | 2026-09-23 | Whose answer the evaluator simulates | After each action of the enemy, the evaluator finds the next character of the party on the timeline. It scores the best action of that character against the enemy side. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Applies D-534 and D-769. The reply follows the real order of the fight, and the cost is the actions of the enemy times the actions of one character. A combination of two characters stays outside the look. The best reply of every character was the other option. Revised in part by D-1101 on 2026-09-25: the best action is the best legal strike, lesson strikes included, and the row term reads their reach. The next character and the share of a kill stand. |
| D-961 | 2026-09-23 | The cost budget of an enemy turn | One enemy turn takes at most 1 ms at the 95th percentile on the Steam Deck. A Tools command times the worst fixture, six enemies against three characters, and reports the 95th percentile of one enemy turn. The PR reports the number of the desktop, and the owner runs the command on the Steam Deck before the merge. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Applies D-161, D-534, F-53, and G-14. Six turns take 6 ms at most, inside the frame of 16.7 ms at 60 frames each second. A miss changes the depth or the profiles in PR-11. A limit of 2 ms, and a report with no limit, were the other options. |
| D-962 | 2026-09-23 | The check of D-948 as a guard | The load check of D-948 stays, and it runs for each entry of each group. Every enemy has the basic attack, the defend, and the step, so no content of PR-11 can fail it. A unit test gives the validator an empty list of actions, and the load fails with the group, the enemy, and the profile. The session found that no profile can be unable to act, and it recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Applies D-359, D-377, D-787, D-948, D-955, and G-21. The check holds a later rule that can empty the list, such as an MP cost of PR-12. Exit test 2 of PR-11 reads the empty list of the unit test. A removal of the check was the other option. |
| D-963 | 2026-09-23 | A column of the waiting enemies, taller than the field (OQ-243) | The load fails a group whose waiting column is taller than the room at the left edge of the field. The error names the group, the height of the column, and the limit. The height of the column is the sum of the heights of the waiting bodies: 32 art pixels for a common enemy, 64 for an elite, and 96 for a boss. The room is the height between the top margin of the frame and the message line, 288 art pixels. Content splits a wave that does not fit. The session found that four elite bodies fit and not six, and it recommended the load failure. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Resolves OQ-243. Applies D-206, D-634, D-953, D-954, G-21, and T-2. A bad group never reaches a player. The limit is 9 common, 4 elite, or 3 boss bodies, or a mix of the same height. Core holds the limit, and a Game test proves that the layout gives the column that room (D-100). A second column to the right, and a column of the next enemies with a count of the rest, were the other options. A body larger than an elite at elite size does not fix the case of five elites, so the session removed that option. Revised in part by D-1035 on 2026-09-24: the room falls from 288 to 270 art pixels. The rule of the room stands. |
| D-964 | 2026-09-23 | A Gitar comment with no item | A Gitar item is a review thread, a finding of the dashboard, or a claim of the CI analysis. The author answers each Gitar item. A Gitar comment with no item, such as a status notice or a clean approval, needs no answer. The reviewer ignores such a comment, and it never blocks a verdict. The prompt of `make codex-review` says the same. The session recommended that neither side act on such a comment, and the owner chose it. | Owner direction, 2026-09-23, in Session 256 of the handoff: "I do NOT want to have to go back and forth with Codex regarding the gitar situation", and the reviewer must "IGNORE unaddressed gitar comments if they do not have specific items that need to be addressed". The review of PR #68 gave `Blocked` for a status notice with no answer alone. Revises D-14, D-66, D-67, D-944, and D-946 in part: each comment with no item needs no answer. A reply to a comment with no item, as the author left on PR #68, stays valid. The stop of D-945 on a thread or a finding stands. The rule outlives the pause of D-945. The author still answers each item after the owner saw it. A reply of the author with the reviewer ignoring an empty comment was the other option. |
| D-965 | 2026-09-23 | The PR of D-964 | PR-98 holds the rule of D-964 beside the waiting enemies. The owner chose it over one concern for each PR (G-8). The session recommended a new PR in the next session. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. An exception to G-8 for PR-98 alone. G-8 stands for every other PR. The rule lands before the merge of PR-98, so its next review reads the new prompt. |

## The character level and MP

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-966 | 2026-09-23 | The shape of a stat curve (OQ-134) | A stat curve is a table with one row for each character level. Each row holds five integers: the health, the MP, the attack, the defense, and the speed. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Resolves OQ-134. Applies D-169, D-537, and G-2. An author tunes each level by hand, and PR-30 changes one number at a time. The load fails a curve with a missing level, or with a stat that falls from one level to the next. A base value and a growth in basis points, and rows at anchor levels with interpolation, were the other options. |
| D-967 | 2026-09-23 | The MP of a rest and of a save point (OQ-135) | A rest at a hub restores all health and all MP. A save point restores all MP, once for the place, and no health. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Resolves OQ-135. Applies D-42, D-389, and D-555. The screen shows no restore number. The level design spaces the save points of a dungeon, because each save point fills every pool once. A part of the MP at a save point, from a number in content, was the other option. |
| D-968 | 2026-09-23 | The shrink of the experience of an enemy (OQ-136) | Each enemy has a level and a base experience in content. For each level that a character stands above the enemy, the experience falls by a cut in basis points. Past a set gap of levels, the enemy gives no experience. The rules file holds the cut and the gap. The session recommended a cut to a floor, and the owner chose a cut, then zero past the gap. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Resolves OQ-136. Applies D-169 and D-388. A character at the level of the enemy, or below it, gets the full base experience. Past the gap, the grind of a region ends. PR-30 tunes the cut and the gap (M-4). A cut to a floor, and zero past a gap with no cut, were the other options. |
| D-969 | 2026-09-23 | The level that the shrink reads (OQ-244) | The shrink reads the level of each character alone. Each character gets the experience of the enemy, cut by the gap between the level of that character and the level of the enemy. The session recommended the average level of the characters on the field, and the owner chose the level of each character. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Resolves OQ-244. Applies D-968. The half of D-73 applies after the shrink. A character who falls behind gets a smaller cut, so the drift of D-73 narrows, and the half of D-73 stands. The summary after a fight shows the experience of each character. |
| D-970 | 2026-09-23 | The recovery of MP in PR-67 | PR-67 adds two rules of the party, with tests: a restore at a save point, which fills MP and no health, and a rest at a hub, which fills health and MP. No map rule calls them yet. PR-16 wires the save point with its record of the used place, and PR-14 wires the rest at a hub. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Applies D-967 and G-8. Exit test 3 of PR-67 becomes a unit test of the rule, and exit test 3 of PR-16 keeps the rule of the used place. The wire of the save point in PR-67 was the other option. |
| D-971 | 2026-09-23 | The experience of each level | One table in the rules file holds the total experience of each character level. The table is the same for every character. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Applies D-34 and D-969. A level means the same thing for each character, so the shrink compares like with like. The characters differ in their stats alone (D-537). A column of experience in each curve was the other option. |
| D-972 | 2026-09-23 | The highest character level | 40. Each curve holds 40 rows, and the experience table holds 40 totals. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Applies D-966 and D-971. The experience of a character stops at the total of level 40. The load fails a curve or a table with another count of rows. 30 and 99 were the other options. |
| D-973 | 2026-09-23 | What a level-up restores | A level-up fills the health and the MP of the character. The session recommended that the health and the MP rise by the gain alone, and the owner chose a fill. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Applies D-34. A level-up feels like a reward. A level-up can act as a free rest, and PR-30 reads that cost against D-35. A down character earns no experience (D-974), so a level-up never meets a down. |
| D-974 | 2026-09-23 | Experience for a downed character | A downed character earns no experience from a battle won, in the party or in the reserve. The owner revised D-387 when the session asked about a level-up of a down character. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Supersedes D-387. Applies D-36 and D-73. A down now costs its share of the fight. A character in reserve keeps the half of D-73. Exit test 2 of PR-67 changes with it. |
| D-975 | 2026-09-23 | The summary after a won fight | The summary shows on the battle screen. Text slides up with a bounce above the head of each character: the experience, such as "+25 XP", then "Level up!" on a level-up, then one line for each stat that rose. The stat lines follow the order health, MP, attack, defense, speed. On a level-up, the health bar and the MP bar of the character fill toward full. A character with no experience shows no text. The loot of PR-13 and PR-65 shows as message lines. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Revises D-835 in part: the form of the summary. | D-1004 | 2026-09-23 | What fires a story scene trigger (OQ-148) | A trigger in the map file has one of four kinds: a step onto a tile, a talk with an NPC, the entry to the map, and the end of a battle. Applies D-528 and D-1001: a story scene after a boss fires with no tile that a player can walk around. Each kind needs its own test and its own place in the tick. A tile and a talk alone were the other option. |

The owner added the pause to the recommended option. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Applies D-162, D-531, and D-540: no enemy reaches the party in a story scene, and a replay reads one rule. The menu stays closed in a story scene. D-1010 places the pause. A map that holds still with the menu open to the player was the other option. |

A battle of a start battle step has no patrol, and its story scene goes on after the win (D-999). An enemy group id was the other option. |
| D-1012 | 2026-09-23 | The path of a move step | A move step lists one direction for each tile. Core checks that each tile is open ground with no other actor, and it sets the end tile. Applies D-106, D-1000, D-1006, and T-2: Core holds no path search, and the author sets the exact path. A tile that blocks the path fails with the story scene, the step, and the tile. A long walk is a long list. A marker in a straight line was the other option. |
| D-1013 | 2026-09-23 | The camera, the show, and the hide | A camera step moves the view to a marker, and it ends on the wait intent. The view goes back to the lead at the end of the story scene. A show step and a hide step change the map at once, and the next step runs in the same tick. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Applies D-1000 and D-1006: each step that Game animates waits, and a step that Game does not animate never waits. A pause after a show takes a wait step. A fade on each show and hide, with a wait intent, was the other option. |
| D-1014 | 2026-09-23 | Who looks a story scene cue up | PR-50 prints no cue. Applies D-545 and T-1: no audio file format exists until PR-38 and PR-70, and no cue exists until PR-72. A cue file of PR-50, and a cue file in Tests alone, were the other options. |

Applies D-57 and G-25: the batch is complete with no pick by hand, and the description stays short. A change of a speaker name alone does not change a story scene. Every story scene in each run, and the story scenes that the session names, were the other options. |
| D-1016 | 2026-09-23 | How the screenplay reaches the PR description | The screenplay tool writes a Screenplay section between two marker lines of a PR body file, and a second run replaces that section. Applies D-514 and G-25: a test proves the insert, the replace, and the refusal with no network, and the Documents section stays intact. A body with no markers gets the section at its end. GitHub refuses a longer body with "Body is too long (maximum is 65536 characters)", as the issues of renovatebot/renovate (#14551) and changesets/action (#174) show, read 2026-09-23. Output to the terminal alone, and a call to `gh` from the tool, were the other options. |

Each other step gives one short action line in italics, and each option of a choose step names its flag. The lead prints as THE LEAD. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-23. Applies D-173 and D-1006: the owner reads the story as a story, and a review names a step by its number. The lead depends on the party order of the run, so the tool prints no name for it. A name comes from the `name.` entry of the cast member in the string table. Dialogue alone, and a table of the steps, were the other options. |

## The lessons

| Id | Date | Topic | Decision | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-1018 | 2026-09-24 | The lesson slots at each level (OQ-137) | A character has two lesson slots at level 1. One more slot opens at each of the levels 5, 12, 20, and 30, so a character has six slots from level 30. The rules file holds the levels. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-24. Resolves OQ-137. Applies D-356 and D-972. Early choices stay tight, and the first playable reaches a third slot soon. PR-30 tunes the levels (G-14). One more slot every 8 levels, three slots at the start, and one slot for each kind were the other options. |
| D-1019 | 2026-09-24 | The points that a lesson gains (OQ-138) | Each equipped lesson gains the experience of each enemy of a won battle, with the shrink of D-968. The shrink reads the lesson, not the character. The half of a reserve character applies after the shrink. The session recommended the experience that the character gets. | Owner answer, 2026-09-24: "If a level 40 character is using a level 1 lesson on a level 10 monster, it shouldn't give 0 xp." Resolves OQ-138. Applies D-73, D-357, D-968, and D-977. A new lesson grows on a strong character, and the grind still ends. D-1020 sets the level that the shrink reads. An experience value of its own for each enemy, and a flat count for each battle, were the other options. |
| D-1020 | 2026-09-24 | The level that the lesson shrink reads | The lesson level is the level that the points of the lesson reach on the experience table of D-971, for that character. The shrink compares the lesson level with the level of the enemy, with the cut and the gap of D-977. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-24. Applies D-361, D-971, D-977, and D-1019. A lesson at level 1 on a character at level 40 gains the full base experience of an enemy at level 10. One table and one shrink rule serve both tracks. No shrink for a lesson, and a cut and a gap of its own, were the other options. |
| D-1021 | 2026-09-24 | Where the points of a lesson stop | The points of a lesson stop at the total that opens the last form of the lesson. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-24. Applies D-539 and D-1019. No progress hides past the last form, and the menu can show that the lesson is complete. A stop at the total of level 40 was the other option. |
| D-1022 | 2026-09-24 | The lessons of a downed character | The lessons of a downed character gain no points from a won battle, in the party or in the reserve. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-24. Revises D-357 in part: a lesson of a downed character gains nothing. The rest of D-357 stands, and a lesson of a character who stands gains points, used or not. Applies D-974: the points of a lesson follow the experience. Half and full points were the other options. |
| D-1023 | 2026-09-24 | Two copies of one lesson (OQ-139) | The player can never own two copies of one lesson. The owner chose this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-24: "Make it impossible for the party to obtain two of a single lesson." Resolves OQ-139. Revises D-365 in part: a shop no longer lets the player own two copies, and content no longer plans for copies. The three sources of D-365 stand. D-361 stands: each character keeps its own points for each lesson it carried. D-1024 sets how the game keeps the rule. The session recommended two copies, with the points of each character. |
| D-1024 | 2026-09-24 | How the game keeps one copy of a lesson | A check at run time. More than one source can list a lesson. A shop hides an owned lesson. A chest that holds an owned lesson gives a fallback item from content. The owned lesson set refuses a second copy with an error. The session recommended one source for each lesson, with a load check. | Owner answer, 2026-09-24. Applies T-2, D-365, and D-1023. An owned lesson is a lesson in the pack, or on a character of the party or the reserve. PR-12 builds the owned lesson set and its refusal. PR-65 hides an owned lesson in a shop, and PR-16 gives the fallback item of a chest. A missed chest can have a shop as a second source. |
| D-1025 | 2026-09-24 | The lessons of Elio after his death | D-364 stands: the lessons and the gear of Elio leave the game with him. At a point after his death, a shop sells one copy of each lesson that left with him, behind a condition. The session recommended that his lessons go back to the party. | Owner answer, 2026-09-24: "Keep D-364: all leaves. We'll need to conditionally sell another copy of each of the lost lessons at some point after Elio dies." Applies D-364, D-543, D-1023, and D-1024. The lost lessons leave the owned lesson set, so the shop check of D-1024 lets the shop sell them. The content after the death of Elio places the shop and its condition. |
| D-1026 | 2026-09-24 | Where a lesson and its forms live | A lesson file of its own, `content/rules/lessons.json`, with the content kind `lesson`. Each lesson holds a name string, an aptitude kind, and its forms. Each form names an ability id of the ability file, its point total, and its MP cost. The first form opens at zero points. PR-12 adds the file. The ability file keeps the effect alone. The session recommended this. | Owner answer, 2026-09-24. Revises D-955 in part: the aptitude and the MP cost sit on the lesson and its forms, not on each entry of the ability file. The effects of D-955 and the ids of D-785 stand. An enemy has no MP, so an enemy move carries no MP cost. Fields on each ability entry were the other option. |
| D-1027 | 2026-09-24 | The forms that a character keeps | A character keeps each form that it opened, and each form is an ability of its own. In a battle, the player picks the lesson name first. | D-1036 | 2026-09-24 | What a piece of gear changes (OQ-140) | A piece of gear adds flat amounts to attack, defense, and speed. A plain piece holds `normal` for all eight elements, and only a very special piece holds another level. The session recommended the five stats and the element table. The owner took health and MP out, and kept the element table for special gear. | Owner answer, 2026-09-24. Resolves OQ-140. Applies D-43, D-374, D-790, and D-794. | D-1037 | 2026-09-24 | How the gear of one character combines (OQ-141) | The stat amounts of all six slots add. For each element, the best protection of any worn piece applies first: `absorb` over `resist` over `normal`. Then each piece with `weak` for that element steps the level down one step, and the floor is `weak`. Two `resist` pieces give `resist`. A `resist` piece and a `weak` piece give `normal`. Two `absorb` pieces and one `weak` piece give `resist`. Five `resist` pieces and one `weak` piece give `normal`. An `absorb` piece and two `weak` pieces give `normal`. | Owner answer, 2026-09-24. Resolves OQ-141. Applies D-44, D-272, and D-1036. The session recommended one priority order, `absorb`, `resist`, `weak`, `normal`. The owner set the step rule: a character with any `weak` piece never has `absorb` for that element, and each `weak` piece counts. Effects that add, and the best level alone, were the other options. |

The load refuses a limit outside the range of its kind. | Owner answer, 2026-09-24. Resolves OQ-142. Applies D-45, D-336, D-382, D-384, D-385, and D-848. A revive stays rare while a cure is common. The session recommended five of each item, and then a range of 1 to 10. The owner chose a limit for each record, with a range for each kind, and a limit for gear too. |

The session set the values from the transcripts of PR #63 to PR #77, where the longest review took 17 minutes. The first CI run on Windows showed that a child of Git Bash runs on outside the process tree and holds the output open, so the read got its own bound. Revises D-926 in part: a run can end at a limit. The rest of D-926 stands. |
